Prompt 1 — Clock-domain and pricing semantics
No hard-evidence findings in this chunk.
Coverage reviewed:
 1. RealizedVolObservation.recv_ts_ms → accepted source state → ReadySourceValue → RealizedVolSnapshot.latest_accepted_receive_ms → classify_rv_gate: Traced end-to-end. The receive watermark is derived at bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:538 from state.samples.back().recv_ts_ms (the latest accepted sample per ready source), aggregated as .max() across ready values at :581-584. Only sources that pass source_diagnostic.ready_value() contribute to ready_values, so only accepted, quorum-contributing observations can advance the watermark. The classifier at bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:443-467 compares only LocalReceiveMs values; as_of_ms is never read for the gate decision.
 2. Fair-value pricing admission and evidence: FairValuePricingRequest.realized_vol_gate_receive_ms is now Option<LocalReceiveMs> at every boundary (current_realized_vol_at, current_surfaced_realized_vol_snapshot_at, fair_value_inputs_at, fair_value_pricing_from_inputs). The compile-time type barrier (LocalReceiveMs vs VenueEventMs are distinct newtypes without cross-type PartialOrd) prevents accidental cross-domain comparison.
 3. Taker entry pricing: on_book_deltas at mod.rs:7715 threads deltas.ts_init.as_u64() / NANOS_PER_MILLI_U64 into try_submit_entry_order_for_receive as LocalReceiveMs. This is the NT process-receive timestamp — the correct clock domain.
 4. Exit pricing paths: 
- book_delta: mod.rs:7698-7703 — BookDelta trigger with Some(deltas.ts_init / NANOS_PER_MILLI_U64) → receive_ms() returns it as LocalReceiveMs. ✓
- signal_quote: mod.rs:1552 — evaluation_receive_ms = received_ts_ms.unwrap_or(clock fallback). In production, signal_quote_from_tick always sets received_ts_ms: Some(quote.ts_init...), so the fallback is unreachable from production. ✓
- selection_update: mod.rs:977-980 — Some(now_ms) where now_ms = snapshot.published_at_ms (strategy clock at selection time). Genuine receive-domain context. ✓
- reference/local: observe_reference_snapshot is #[cfg(test)] only; production reference updates via on_data → observe_reference_price_update do NOT trigger exit evaluation. ✓
 5. Maker pricing: bolt_v3_maker_runtime_quote.rs:167 uses selected_quote.received_ts_ms() (returns u64, non-optional) wrapped as LocalReceiveMs. ReferenceQuote.received_ts_ms() is defined at bolt_v3_reference_price.rs:879.
 6. Classification ordering (classify_rv_gate): MissingSnapshot → MissingEvaluationEventTime (eval receive None) → RejectedNotReady (snapshot receive watermark None) → RejectedFutureDated → RejectedStale → RejectedNotReady (ready_realized_vol None) → Accepted. A structurally absent receive stamp on the evaluation side returns MissingEvaluationEventTime (fail-closed). A snapshot with latest_accepted_receive_ms: None returns RejectedNotReady before freshness checks — correct because ready snapshots always have accepted samples with receive timestamps, so None implies not-ready.
 7. Rejected observations: latest_accepted_event_ts() (bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:469-477) uses only state.samples.back() — the last_rejected_event_ts_ms contribution was removed. publish_surface_after_routed_observation (bolt_v3_realized_volatility_runtime.rs:388-398) derives as_of_ms from latest_accepted_event_ts() only. A rejected first observation falls through to VenueEventMs::new(u64::MIN), producing a not-ready snapshot. Proven by rejected_routed_observation_does_not_advance_the_surface_watermark.
 8. Snapshot replacement ordering: observe_realized_vol_snapshot uses current.as_of_ms <= snapshot.as_of_ms (unchanged), so equal-timestamp republish replaces. A rejected-observation republish produces the same as_of_ms and same latest_accepted_receive_ms (accepted-only), so the replacement preserves the watermark while updating diagnostics.
 9. Reference-price event-time freshness preserved: TakerPricingRequest.reference_gate_event_ms: Option<VenueEventMs> feeds reference_current_price_stale_at via current_reference_pricing_event_ms() (renamed from current_pricing_venue_event_ms(), body unchanged). The RV gate and reference gate now use separate typed fields. The fair_value_pricing_does_not_compare_independent_venue_clocks differential proves event-domain as_of no longer affects the RV gate.
10. No production trigger reaches MissingEvaluationEventTime: Verified all production trigger sites — on_book_deltas, on_quote → after_signal_quote_observed, apply_selection_snapshot. All pass Some(receive_ms). Only BoltV3ExitTriggerSource::Other with None can reach it (test-only structural absence, exit_evaluation_evidence_holds_when_receive_time_is_structurally_absent).
11. Event-domain diagnostics preserved: rv_as_of_minus_now_ms and rv_future_dating_delta_ms use diagnostic_event_ms (= trigger_context.venue_event_ms(), unchanged method using ts_event_ms). These are evidence-only fields.
12. Blast radius: Entry may price on previously cross-venue-blocked ticks (book ts_init feeds the gate). Normal exit may compute hold EV on those ticks. Maker may be available. Forced-flat (position_forced_flat_reasons_at — independent of RV gate), not-ready, stale, and missing-snapshot paths remain fail-closed. All consistent with the PR body.
Evidence basis: Exact-head source inspection at 2b83f51, base comparison at 9ac211f, full diff read (2,412 lines), type system verification at bolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs, all production DataActor handler call sites traced.
Risk areas considered: Equal-timestamp replacement ordering, accepted-only multi-source watermark max, u64::MIN fallback for empty surfaces, saturating_duration_since overflow safety, receive-domain future-dated boundary, cross-source event/receive divergence under single-threaded event ordering, test fixture value adjustments (1_180→1_220 — necessary because receive_ms() uses ts_init_ms where old venue_event_ms() used ts_event_ms; the old gate never read ts_init_ms).
Prompt 2 — Evidence flood and recovery capacity
No hard-evidence findings in this chunk.
Coverage reviewed:
1. ExitOutcomeKey construction (exit_decision.rs:329-333): Unchanged struct {exit_decision, submission_blocked_reason, rv_gate_result}. The dedupe key is NOT modified. The flood is eliminated because rv_gate_result is now stable (Accepted) within the receive domain, rather than oscillating Accepted ↔ RejectedFutureDated from cross-venue event clocks. This is a root-cause fix, not a dedupe-key patch.
2. Every classifier call used by exit behavior: 
- exit_evaluation_for_trigger_at → exit_evaluation_with_rv_gate_at(now_ms, trigger_context.receive_ms()) — gate uses receive domain.
- record_exit_evaluation_evidence → classify_realized_vol_gate(surface_id, rv_gate_receive_ms, max_age) — same receive stamp.
- exit_realized_volatility_gate_fields_at(realized_vol_gate_receive_ms, diagnostic_event_ms) — gate uses receive, diagnostics use event.
All three call sites are consistent.
3. Alternating book_delta timestamps: exit_evaluation_dedupe_ignores_alternating_consuming_venue_clock_lead feeds six ticks with event_ts alternating 1_200/1_199 and monotonically increasing ts_init 1_210+index. Old code: venue_event_ms() returned ts_event_ms → oscillated Accepted/RejectedFutureDated → 6 keys → 6 records. New code: receive_ms() returns ts_init_ms → all Accepted → 1 key → 1 record. Genuine differential.
4. Interleaved book/signal/selection: exit_evaluation_dedupe_does_not_oscillate_across_trigger_sources feeds BookDelta, SelectionUpdate, SignalQuote, SelectionUpdate. Old code: SelectionUpdate returned None from venue_event_ms() → MissingEvaluationEventTime interleaved with Accepted → ≥2 keys → 4 records. New code: all carry Some(receive_ms) → stable Accepted → 1 record.
5. rv_as_of_minus_now_ms remains diagnostic only: Computed from diagnostic_event_ms.zip(...) at mod.rs:6781. Stored in evidence record. Not read by any gate or decision path.
6. position_id=None per-tick writer: The flood guard at mod.rs:6811 is if let Some(position_id) = log_fields.position_id — bypassed when None. Unchanged by this PR. Disclosed in PR body as report-only on #1354. With the RV flap fixed, the position_id=Some path collapses; the position_id=None path remains one-shot in the audited terminal-release path.
7. Rejected RV observations poisoning later publication watermark: publish_surface_after_routed_observation now uses latest_accepted_event_ts() (accepted-only). latest_accepted_receive_ms is derived from ready_values only. The test proves a ts=50_000 rejected observation leaves both as_of_ms and latest_accepted_receive_ms at 4_000.
8. Root-cause vs dedupe-key: The PR does NOT modify ExitOutcomeKey, any dedupe logic, or any flood guard. It stops the behavioral flap by making the classifier input monotonic (receive domain).
Archived-session counterfactual assessment:
The interpretation is valid. The 47,551-byte open-position window over 939.35s represents 4 genuine exit-evaluation transitions (2 exit_hold entries, 2 position_interval_ended entries) plus 9 exit decisions and 1 booking error. After the four phase transitions, unchanged holding time adds zero exit-evaluation records because the ExitOutcomeKey is stable. The 182,235.53 bytes/hour is therefore a transition cost amortized over the observed window, not a continuing per-tick rate.
The first-restart counterfactual (199,023 bytes) leaves 849,553 bytes of headroom under the 1 MiB reader limit. A planned restart could still cross 1 MiB through legitimate lifecycle accumulation if enough position cycles complete before the restart. The PR explicitly states this and does not bundle rotation/segmented recovery. #1275/#763 must become pre-soak work if the soak schedules its first restart after multiple completed position lifecycles.
Recovery tests:
restart_with_open_position_recovers_at_one_mib_boundary and restart_with_open_position_enters_blind_recovery_above_one_mib_boundary:
- Create a real Position via Position::new(&instrument, fill) with PositionId::from("P-RECOVERY-BOUNDARY") and add it to the cache via cache.add_position.
- Write real files via std::fs::write at exactly 1,048,576 and 1,048,577 bytes.
- Call bootstrap_recovery_from_cache() which reads the file through the configured recovery path.
- The over-limit test MUST exercise the real reader: BlindRecovery(SettlementEvidenceRecoveryFailed) is only reachable through the reader rejecting the oversized file. If the reader weren't called, the cached position would produce Managed regardless.
- TEST_RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_MAX_BYTES = 1_048_576 matches the production limit cited in issue #1354.
- No tolerance windows, caps, sampling, larger recovery limit, or rotation implementation present.
Evidence basis: Full record_exit_evaluation_evidence and try_submit_exit_order_for_trigger call chains traced; ExitOutcomeKey struct verified unchanged; archived-session arithmetic verified (47,551 × 3600 / 939.353592 ≈ 182,235.53); recovery test harness inspected at adverse_path_harness.rs:2342-2430.
Risk areas considered: Dedupe key integrity across trigger sources, rejected-observation watermark poisoning, position_id=None guard bypass, counterfactual amortization interpretation validity, multi-lifecycle 1 MiB accumulation, recovery test real-file-read proof.

   Findings

   Finding 1 — Entry path evaluates the RV gate under two different receive stamps within one evaluation
   (Prompts 1 & 3)

   Severity: Low

   Evidence:
   • src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/mod.rs:7715 threads LocalReceiveMs::new(deltas.ts_init…)
     into the entry decision as evaluation_receive_ms.
   • entry_evaluation_for_receive_at (mod.rs:7192) uses that stamp for pricing inputs (mod.rs:7221) and
     fair probability (mod.rs:7231) — but mod.rs:7307, 7399, and 7491 call
     current_uncertainty_band_probability_at(now_ms, …), which re-gates RV at
     Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms)) (mod.rs:3836). On rejection the decision pushes
     EntryPricingBlockReason::UncertaintyBandUnavailable and clears selected_side/sized_notional.
   • entry_strategy_input_evidence_snapshot_at (mod.rs:6407) requires
     current_realized_vol_for_gate_at(Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms))) and bail!s the whole submit when
     it returns None ("entry strategy input evidence requires realized volatility").
   • entry_evaluation_log_fields_at (mod.rs:3891) logs realized_vol gated at now_ms (tracing-only).
   • Pre-change every one of these sites consumed the single current_pricing_venue_event_ms() value, so
     decision and evidence could not disagree; the PR threaded ts_init only partway down the entry path.
     The exit path is fully consistent (every consumer takes trigger_context.receive_ms()).
   • Window sizing: now_ms ≥ ts_init always; with max_source_age_ms = 2000
     (config/strategies/binary_oracle_*.toml:69), divergence requires the receive watermark's age at
     ts_init to land in (2000 − (now_ms−ts_init), 2000] — a few ms wide at the staleness boundary. Effect
     is one-directional and safe (blocks or drops an admitted entry for one tick, never wrong-admits;
     self-heals on the next book delta), but durable entry evidence can disagree with the decision it
     explains, and this behavior change is not disclosed in the PR body.

   Issue: One entry evaluation consumes RV freshness under two receive timestamps, so the
   uncertainty-band check and the submit-time evidence builder can veto an admission the same evaluation
   just made.

   Fix / verification: Thread evaluation_receive_ms through current_uncertainty_band_probability_at /
   adjusted_probability_up_for_fee_uncertainty, entry_strategy_input_evidence_snapshot_at, and
   entry_evaluation_log_fields_at. Add a differential test with the watermark age between the two stamps
   (e.g. ts_init=T, now_ms=T+5, watermark age at T just under max_source_age) asserting decision and
   evidence admit identically.

   Finding 2 — Plan doc misstates where the recovery-boundary tests live (Prompt 3)

   Severity: Low

   Evidence: docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-rv-gate-clock-domain.md Task 6 names
   tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs as a test file; the diff adds the 1 MiB boundary tests only in
   src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/tests/adverse_path_harness.rs and never touches that file.
   Durable repo doc now contradicts the merged tree.

   Issue: Plan/implementation drift in a committed document.

   Fix / verification: Align Task 6 with the actual test location.

   Prompt 2 explicit flag — #1275/#763 pre-soak determination

   The counterfactual arithmetic checks out: 47,551 × 3600 / 939.353592 = 182,235.53 bytes/hour, and
   1,048,576 − 199,023 = 849,553. The "transition cost, not continuing growth" interpretation is valid:
   the 14 retained open-window records are 4 exit transitions + 9 exit decisions + 1 terminal booking
   error, and unchanged holding adds zero records, so the per-hour figure decays with holding time rather
   than continuing linearly.

   Flag: at ~199 KB per lifecycle of this shape, 1,048,576 / 199,023 ≈ 5.3 — the 1 MiB whole-file bound
   is crossed after ~6 lifecycles, i.e. ~1.5 hours of soaking on a 15-minute cadence. Unless the soak
   schedules its first open-position restart within ~5 lifecycles of a fresh evidence file, the restart
   leg will enter BlindRecovery(SettlementEvidenceRecoveryFailed). So for any realistic soak schedule,
   segmented recovery/rotation under #1275/#763 is pre-soak work; the PR body states this conditionally,
   and I confirm the dependency. This PR correctly does not bundle it (no tolerance, cap, sampling,
   limit, or rotation change exists in the diff — the production limit stays config-driven at
   config/root.toml:195).

   The restart tests are genuine: restart_strategy_with_open_position_at_recovery_path builds a real
   cached Position in the NT cache, and bootstrap_recovery_from_cache →
   recover_settlement_bootstrap_from_scope → read_settlement_keys_for_recovery_scope runs the real
   whole-file reader (take(max_bytes+1), error above bound). Exactly 1,048,576 bytes recovers to Managed;
   1,048,577 enters BlindRecovery(SettlementEvidenceRecoveryFailed) through the production path — no
   mock.

   Everything else in Prompt 2 audits clean: ExitOutcomeKey (exit_decision, submission_blocked_reason,
   receive-domain rv_gate_result) is stable across venue-clock skew; the alternating-book (6→1) and
   interleaved-trigger (4→1) differentials compile against the pre-change API and genuinely fail there;
   selection_update carries genuine local receive context (published_at_ms from the local clock);
   rv_as_of_minus_now_ms is diagnostic-only and absent from the outcome key; position_id=None is
   reachable only after terminal settlement releases exposure inside the same call (one-shot, every
   production trigger call site gates on a managed position — cannot become a per-tick writer); rejected
   observations update only last_rejected_* diagnostics and cannot advance either watermark; and the flap
   is removed at the root (the classifier itself no longer compares cross-venue clocks), not merely
   re-keyed.

Prompt 1 — Clock-domain and pricing semantics

The ownership change is real and mostly correct: classify_rv_gate is the single source of truth for both the pricing admission (current_surfaced_realized_vol_snapshot_at admits only on Accepted) and the #885 exit evidence, and it now compares only LocalReceiveMs against LocalReceiveMs. LocalReceiveMs
deliberately has no signed_delta_sinceg, and the compile_fail doctest inbolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs pins that. But the entry path is only half-converted.

Finding 1 — Entry admission still gatehe strategy clock, defeating the PR's

Severity: High.

Evidence: In binary_oracle_edge_taker::mod.rs, entry_evaluation_for_receive_at(now_ms, evaluation_receive_ms) threads the receive stamp into two of its three realized-vol consumers:

- current_entry_pricing_inputs_for_receive_at(now_ms, evaluation_receive_ms) → gates at
evaluation_receive_ms
- current_fair_probability_up_for_receive_at(now_ms, evaluation_receive_ms) → gates at evaluation_receive_ms
- current_uncertainty_band_probability_at(now_ms, …) → gates at Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms))

adjusted_probability_up_for_fee_uncertainty (called from the executable_edge_for_side probes, which run earlier on the same path) does the same. entry_evaluation_log_fields_at and entry_strategy_input_evidence_snapshot_at also use Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms)).

At base 9ac211fe1 all of these used onvenue_event_ms(). This PR split them.
The only production entry caller is on LocalReceiveMs::new(deltas.ts_init …)as evaluation_receive_ms while now_ms is read from self.clock() after the handler prologue, so now_ms ≥
deltas.ts_init always.

Issue: Let W be the snapshot's latest_accepted_receive_ms, D = deltas.ts_init, N = now_ms, and max_source_age_ms = 2000 (config/strategies/binary_oracle_*.toml:69). The pricing gate admits when D − W ≤ 2000; the uncertainty band admits when N − W ≤ 2000. Because the band is on the admit path and is
strictly stricter, the binding entry gading ts_init into entry pricing has no re-emits a strategy_input_snapshot. That is the same record kind that contributed 125,144 of the incident's 166,086 records.

The submit-linked snapshot is not at risk: an admitted entry implies the band passed at now_ms, so entry_strategy_input_evidence_snapshot_at's realized-vol lookup at now_ms also succeeds, and its ? sits before submit_order_with_decision_evidence. No order can be stranded. The defect is a wrong-clock admission gate plus a latch reset, not a lost submit-linked record.

Fix / verification: Thread evaluation__uncertainty_band_probability_at,l_decimal.is_some(). Both publish_surface_after_routed_observation and refresh_surface_at derive as_of_ms from latest_accepted_event_ts().or(last_refresh_ms), and last_refresh_ms is only ever assigned from an accepted-derived as_of, so there is no path by which a rejected observation poisons a later publication. Proven and rejected diagnostics remain visible in source_diagnostics.
- Multi-source aggregation. The watermark is max over ready sources. This is causally valid because per-source staleness is enforced upstream in the event domain: source_diagnostic computes coverage/gap against as_of_ms and a lagging source drops out of Ready, so every contributor to the max is itself
fresh. It also preserves the prior maxntics.
- No production trigger reaches MissingEvaluationEventTime. The four production exit triggers are SelectionUpdate (Some(now_ms)), SignalQuote (Some(received_ts_ms.unwrap_or(clock now))), BookDelta (Some(deltas.ts_init)), and — importantly — ReferenceUpdate is #[cfg(test)] (it lives inside
observe_reference_snapshot, which is tluationTriggerContext::unknown andtry_submit_exit_order. Entry always passes Some(evaluation_receive_ms); maker always passes Some(...).
- Selection updates carry genuine receive context. ts_init_ms = now_ms is the NT strategy clock, i.e. process-local — not a relabeled venue event. And venue_event_ms() returns None for
SelectionUpdate/Unknown/Other, so the nto the venue-event diagnostics.eject.
- Reference-price event-time freshness preserved. TakerPricingRequest gained a separate reference_gate_event_ms, fed by the renamed current_reference_pricing_event_ms() (formerly current_pricing_venue_event_ms), and it still flows to reference_current_price_stale_at in the VenueEventMs domain. Behavior unchanged.
- Equal timestamps. snapshot_receive_ms > eval is strict, so equal receive stamps accept — which is the common case, since on_quote routes a spot tick into the surface and then evaluates the signal-quote trigger with that same tick's ts_init. publish_surface_at(..., allow_equal_timestamp = true) on both
publish paths, so equal-stamp republicent.
- Cross-venue event timestamps never decide admission. rv_as_of_minus_now_ms and future_dating_delta_ms are now computed from diagnostic_event_ms = trigger_context.venue_event_ms() against snapshot.as_of_ms — same domain, and neither is in ExitOutcomeKey.

Residual risk (no hard evidence, not filed as a finding): the receive domain assumes NT dispatches to the actor in ts_init order. If a spot tick is stamped ≥1 ms after a book delta yet reaches the actor first, the watermark exceeds deltas.ts_init and the gate returns RejectedFutureDated. That is
fail-closed (hold) and I found no systms inversion across a FIFO bus, but it
is worth a soak assertion: count receited and expect ~0.

Prompt 2 — Evidence flood and recovery capacity

The flood fix is real, not a key changched by this PR — still {exit_decision,he snapshot into pricing. So the pre-change Accepted → RejectedFutureDated → Accepted oscillation was withholding realized volatility from real pricing, not merely mislabelling evidence; fixing the domain stops the behavioral admission flap and the dedupe collapse follows from it. The two new tests match the claimed counts: alternating book_delta event timestamps 1_200/1_199 across six evaluations → records.len() == 1; interleaved book/selection/signal → records.len() == 1. Both are genuinely differential (old code: the alternating consuming clock flips future-dated on odd ticks → 6; SelectionUpdate returned venue_event_ms() == None → MissingEvaluationEventTime alternating with Accepted → 4).
fact path, and the PR ships no replay harness for it, so the record counts are author-reported and unverified by me. The internal arithmetic does check out: 4 + 9 + 93 = 106; 40,849 + 125,144 + 93 = 166,086; 47,551 × 3600 ÷ 939.353592 = 182,235.53. Taking the numbers as given:

- Retained, all kinds: 199,023 bytes for a 24-minute session containing one position lifecycle.
- 1,048,576 ÷ 199,023 ≈ 5.3 such sessions.
- Even on the most generous accounting — treating all 93 "other-kind" records as one-shot startup evidence — the marginal per-lifecycle cost is the 47,551-byte open-position window plus the ~50,931
bytes of blocked-entry snapshots accruat reaches 1 MiB in ~10 lifecycles.ugh completed lifecycles to cross that bound…") is effectively unconditional at soak scale; satisfying it by restarting early converts an acceptance criterion into a scheduling artifact.

Fix / verification: Not in this PR — the hard constraints are correct and respected (no tolerance window, cap, sampling, larger recovery limit, or rotation is present in this diff; git diff over config/, ci/, scripts/, .github/, justfile, Cargo.* is empty). The action is a scope call: #1275/#763 (segmented recovery or rotation) must be scheduled as pre-soak work, and the PR body's capacity section
should say so unconditionally rather tfigure as reassurance. Verification fortstrap_from_scope early-returns true when recovery_scope_settlement_keys.is_empty(), so the fact that the 1,048,577-byte case reaches BlindRecovery(SettlementEvidenceRecoveryFailed) proves the scope was non-empty and the reader ran; the 1,048,576-byte case reaching Managed proves the cap did not trip. The boundary is exactly as specified. One fidelity gap worth noting: the fixture is vec![b'\n'; N], so it validates the size gate but not that a 1 MiB file of real records recovers — given that capacity is the operational crux, a fixture built from the counterfactual's retained record mix at exactly 1 MiB would be the stronger artifact.

selection_update now carries genuine rstrategy clock), not a relabeled eventtimestamp, and rv_as_of_minus_now_ms remains event-domain and diagnostic-only.

Prompt 3 — Rust/API, tests, and scope

Type ownership is enforced at the boun the RV gate everywhere(FairValuePricingRequest.realized_vol_gate_receive_ms, TakerPricingRequest.realized_vol_gate_receive_ms, RealizedVolSnapshot.latest_accepted_receive_ms, ReadySourceValue.latest_accepted_receive_ms), VenueEventMs retained for the reference gate and the RCA diagnostics. Classification ordering is MissingSnapshot → MissingEvaluationEventTime → RejectedNotReady (no watermark) → RejectedFutureDated → RejectedStale → RejectedNotReady (not ready) → Accepted, which is fail-closed at every step. Borrowing, saturation (saturating_duration_since), equal timestamps, and the u64::MIN fallback all behave. No cap, tolerance, sampling, byte-limit, venue hardcode, or unrelated dedupe change appears anywhere in the
diff. The two docs land in docs/superps 16 such files at base — establishedconvention, not scope creep.

Finding 3 — Maker gates realized-vol freshness at the reference quote's receive time, which systematically future-dates a fresher RV; the new test pins only the passing orientation

Severity: Medium (no live blast radius#488 will build on).

Evidence: bolt_v3_maker_runtime_quote.t_price_fair_value_decision:
let request = FairValuePricingRequest {
    now_ms,
    realized_vol_gate_receive_ms: Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(selected_quote.received_ts_ms())),               …
_receive_ms advances on every accepted spot tick. selected_quote.received_ts_ms() advances only when a new reference report arrives (Chainlink stamps it with current_unix_timestamp_ms() at WS frame receipt — genuinely local, so the domain is right, but the reference point is wrong). Any evaluation at which spot has ticked since the last reference report gives snapshot_receive_ms > eval_receive_ms → RejectedFutureDated → RealizedVolNotReady → maker blocked. Reference-price staleness is already enforced by the selector's own max-age; the RV gate has no reason to adopt the quote's stamp. The new test hides this: reference_quote(asset, source, price, ts) sets observed_ts_ms == received_ts_ms == 1_000 and the snapshot is pinned to latest_accepted_receive_ms = 999, i.e. RV strictly older than the reference quote — the one orientation that passes. Flip it (RV receive 1_001 vs          reference received 1_000) and the makey is nil:ation_… asserts as_of stays 4_000 where the old publish_surface_after_routed_observation(surface_id, 50_000) produced 50_000; the two dedupe tests reproduce the 6→1 and 4→1 collapses. They cannot be literally compiled at base (the API shape changed), which is inherent to a type-ownership fix — the author's RED transcript is consistent with the old logic on every line.
- Fixture fidelity gap (worth folding into the fix for Finding 1): the strategy-level exit tests seed through FairValuePricingState::seed_ready_realized_vol, which is #[cfg(test)] and sets latest_accepted_receive_ms = Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(ready_ts_ms)) — collapsing the two domains. No strategy-level test therefore exercises the production shape where recv_ts_ms > event_ts_ms by the feed latency. Seeding as_of = 1_200, receiv tests actually discriminate.
- .expect("a ready source must contain an accepted sample") in snapshot_at is structurally unreachable, so I am not filing it: source_status returns Ready iff computation.rv.is_some(); compute_rv needs ≥2    grid points; grid_prices yields nothin ⟹ samples non-empty.
- Author's verification claims, independently checked: full unfiltered local nextest on my own checkout at 2b83f512a → 3354 tests run: 3354 passed, 0 skipped (matches the claimed 3,354). CI run 29147575691 → conclusion: success, headSha: 2b83f512c73e97 (matches). PR checks show test,source-fence-static, bvs-fmt, detector, ci-policy all SUCCESS at head. Clippy and just fmt-check I did not re-run locally; CI's green bvs-fmt and the workflow's warnings-denied clippy cover them.
- Undisclosed behavior change: none found beyond Finding 1, which the body implicitly mis-states ("Book entry/exit uses NT ts_init" is true for exit, not for entry admission).

Bottom line                                                                                             
Finding 1 is the one I'd block on: it is a live-path correctness defect in the exact seam this PR is establishing, it silently nullifies the entry half of the headline change, and it can reset the #1354 blocked-snapshot dedupe latch. Finding 3 is a cheap one-line fix plus a test. Finding 2 is not a defect in this diff but is a hard scheduling  before the soak — the restart leg willhit BlindRecovery on a file that grew ifecycle evidence, and nothing in thisPR prevents that.
GPT:  ## Prompt 1 — clock-domain and pricing semantics

  Severity: Medium
  Evidence: RealizedVolEngine::snapshot_at computes each source’s RV from samples filtered to event_ts_ms
  <= as_of_ms through source_diagnostic, but assigns ReadySourceValue.latest_accepted_receive_ms from
  state.samples.back() without the same cutoff (src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:520-543). Therefore, an
  accepted sample newer than the requested surface as_of_ms can supply the receive watermark even though
  it did not contribute to that snapshot’s computation.
  Issue: This refutes the invariant that only observations contributing to the published surface advance
  its receive watermark. Such a snapshot can be incorrectly classified RejectedFutureDated or
  RejectedStale. Normal routed publication currently requests the latest accepted event watermark,
  limiting production exposure, but snapshot_at is public and the requested-as-of behavior is semantically
  wrong.
  Fix / verification: Derive the receive watermark from the latest accepted sample at or before as_of_ms
  for each ready source—ideally from the exact filtered sample set used by source_diagnostic. Add a
  differential test with a ready historical surface plus an accepted sample whose event time is newer than
  the requested as_of_ms, proving the newer sample cannot affect the historical snapshot’s receive
  watermark.

  Otherwise, the traced production paths support the intended ownership change:

  - Rejected observations remain in diagnostics but cannot advance latest_accepted_event_ts or routed
    publication’s causal watermark.

  - Multi-source publication takes the maximum receive stamp across ready quorum-counting sources.
  - Entry uses exact book ts_init; normal exit uses trigger receive context; signal falls back to the
    process clock when its receive stamp is absent; selection uses its local clock; maker uses
    ReferenceQuote.received_ts_ms.

  - All identified production exit triggers construct Some(ts_init_ms); the missing-receive route remains
    fail-closed and test-only.

  - Cross-venue event timestamps remain computation/evidence inputs, including rv_as_of_minus_now_ms, and
    do not drive RV admission.

  - Receive-domain future and stale classifications remain fail-closed.
  - Taker reference-price freshness remains separately event-time based through reference_gate_event_ms.
  - Forced-flat decisions precede normal RV-dependent exit valuation, preserving their safety behavior;
    not-ready RV continues to block entry/normal exit pricing.

  ## Prompt 2 — evidence flood and recovery capacity

  No hard-evidence findings in this chunk.

  Coverage reviewed:

  - ExitOutcomeKey construction and classifier use by exit behavior, tracing fields, and durable
    exit_evaluation.

  - Six alternating book_delta event timestamps collapsing to one record.
  - Interleaved book_delta, selection_update, and signal_quote collapsing to one record.
  - Selection receive context, signal receive fallback, missing-receive behavior, position_id=None,
    rejected-observation publication, and behavioral pricing admission.

  - Exact 1 MiB and 1 MiB+1 recovery tests.
  - Archived-session arithmetic and lifecycle accumulation.

  Evidence basis:

  - rv_gate_result is now classified from receive timestamps before entering ExitOutcomeKey; trigger event
    timestamps remain outside the key.

  - The differential pricing tests independently demonstrate removal of the admission flap, rather than
    merely changing dedupe.

  - Recovery tests register a real cached open Position, invoke bootstrap_recovery_from_cache, and
    exercise the configured whole-file recovery path. Exactly 1,048,576 bytes reaches Managed; 1,048,577
    bytes reaches BlindRecovery(SettlementEvidenceRecoveryFailed).

  - The arithmetic is correct: 47,551 × 3,600 / 939.353592 = 182,235.53 bytes/hour. Describing this as
    transition cost is valid for unchanged holding time because stable outcomes no longer append per tick.

  Risk areas considered:

  - The counterfactual supports the stated first restart only if it occurs from a fresh file after a
    comparable lifecycle: 199,023 bytes leaves 849,553 bytes.

  - Legitimate completed lifecycles still accumulate permanently. Roughly 18 additional comparable 47,551-
    byte lifecycle windows would consume that remaining margin, potentially sooner when other lifecycle
    evidence is included.

  - Therefore, #1275/#763 must become pre-soak work if the planned restart can occur after enough
    completed trades to exceed 1 MiB. This PR correctly does not implement rotation, segmentation, caps,
    sampling, tolerance, or a larger limit.

  - position_id=None remains one-shot after terminal exposure release in the audited route, not a per-tick
    writer.

Prompt 1 — clock-domain and pricing semantics

End-to-end path (mostly holds)

┌────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Step                   │ Behavior at head                                           │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ RealizedVolObservation │ Stored only when observe accepts; rejects update last_     │
│ .recv_ts_ms            │ rejected_* only                                            │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Accepted → samples     │ Quorum-ready sources put LocalReceiveMs(sample.recv_ts_ms) │
│                        │ on ReadySourceValue                                        │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Aggregate watermark    │ max(ready_values.latest_accepted_receive_ms) →             │
│                        │ RealizedVolSnapshot.latest_accepted_receive_ms             │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ classify_rv_gate       │ Compares only evaluation LocalReceiveMs vs snapshot        │
│                        │ receive watermark; as_of_ms is not used for admission      │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Fair value / taker     │ realized_vol_gate_receive_ms: LocalReceiveMs; reference    │
│                        │ staleness is separate reference_gate_event_ms:             │
│                        │ VenueEventMs                                               │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Entry (book)           │ LocalReceiveMs::new(deltas.ts_init / 1e6)                  │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Exit book / signal     │ ts_init / signal receive (clock fallback if signal receive │
│                        │ missing)                                                   │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Exit selection / local │ Some(now_ms) strategy clock as receive context             │
├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Maker                  │ LocalReceiveMs::new(selected_quote.received_ts_ms())       │
└────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Proved:

• Rejected observations do not enter samples → cannot advance accepted event watermark (latest_accepted_event_ts) or receive watermark; diagnostics remain visible (routed_quote_rejection_*, rejected_routed_observation_* tests).
• Multi-source ready watermark is max over ready, quorum-counting sources only (skewed non-ready B does not move watermark).
• Production exit/entry/maker/selection/local all supply a receive stamp; structural None remains MissingEvaluationEventTime + Hold.
• Cross-venue event clocks stay diagnostic (rv_as_of_minus_now_ms / future-dating delta from venue_event_ms() only).
• Reference-price event freshness is split and preserved (reference_gate_event_ms + current_reference_pricing_event_ms).
• Receive-domain future/stale still fail closed in classify_rv_gate.

Findings

Severity: Medium
Evidence: entry_evaluation_for_receive_at admits fair value with evaluation_receive_ms (book ts_init), then calls current_uncertainty_band_probability_at(now_ms, …) which re-gates RV as LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms) (strategy clock). Same dual stamp appears in entry_evaluation_log_fields_at and entry_strategy_input_evidence_snapshot_at.
Issue: Entry pricing is not single-clock. Fair-value RV uses the evaluation receive stamp; uncertainty-band (required for any successful entry evaluation after fair value) uses strategy now_ms. Near max_source_age_ms, book-receive can Accept while strategy-clock Rejects → false UncertaintyBandUnavailable (fail-closed but wrong ownership). Cross-venue flap is gone, but receive-domain ownership is incomplete for entry.
Fix / verification: Thread evaluation_receive_ms into uncertainty-band (and entry log/evidence RV fields). Differential: admit at ts_init with watermark W and now_ms = W + max_age + 1 while ts_init = W + max_age → must still use one stamp for both arms.

Severity: Low
Evidence: RealizedVolEngine::snapshot_at sets each ready source’s receive stamp from state.samples.back(), not the last sample with event_ts_ms <= as_of_ms used for RV math. Production publish uses as_of = latest_accepted_event_ts(), so back samples are always ≤ as_of; the public snapshot_at(as_of) API can still stamp a non-contributing newer sample.
Issue: Watermark ownership is “latest accepted sample in buffer,” not strictly “latest sample that contributed to this as_of surface.” Design wording claims contributing observations.
Fix / verification: Take max recv_ts_ms over samples with event_ts_ms <= as_of_ms (or last filtered sample). Test snapshot_at(older_as_of) after a newer accepted sample.

No hard-evidence findings on: rejected poisoning of production publish watermarks; maker receive path; selection/local receive context; missing-receive fail-closed; reference event-time preservation; equal-timestamp sample replace; runtime reject-republish diagnostics.

Blast radius (as implemented): Entry/exit/maker can price on ticks formerly blocked only by cross-venue event order (subject to the entry dual-clock caveat). Forced-flat, not-ready, stale, missing snapshot, and absent receive remain fail-closed. Historical gate enum labels preserved; event deltas diagnostic-only.

• Recommendation: REQUEST CHANGES. The amendment is directionally correct, but four design gaps remain.
  dd1c5c4c9 changes only the design document over 2b83f512a; that is expected for this design review.

  ### Findings

  1. High — “Eligible before cutoff” is not the same as “actually contributed.”

     Evidence: Design lines 16–17 require the latest contributing observation, but lines 70–73 define the
     watermark using accepted observations at or before as_of_ms. grid_prices selects only observations
     used at grid points (src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:919–948); non-divisible window/interval
     configurations are allowed (:631–635).

     Issue: An accepted observation can be before as_of_ms but after the final grid point, changing the
     watermark without changing RV. That can falsely refresh or future-date unchanged pricing data.

     Required correction: Preserve sample identity through every base/coarse/subsample grid and calculate
     the watermark from the observations actually used by the published computation. Add a non-divisible-
     grid differential. If “all eligible observations” is intended instead, change the invariant and
     justify why unused data refreshes pricing.

  2. High — The function-signature ownership claim is not enforced.

     Evidence: The design retains optional stamps in FairValuePricingRequest and TakerPricingRequest at
     lines 47–55, while lines 59–76 claim every evaluation necessarily owns one stamp and that signatures
     enforce this. Current structs confirm the optional channel at src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:36–41
     and src/bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs:45–51.

     Issue: Production pricing can still be constructed with None, and helpers can still receive both
     now_ms and an optional stamp and choose the wrong one.

     Required correction: Make production pricing requests require LocalReceiveMs. Preserve
     MissingEvaluationEventTime only in a separate diagnostic/classifier boundary. RV-consuming helpers
     should accept only the required stamp; lifecycle code may separately retain now_ms.

  3. High — One entry differential cannot prove every independent re-gate.

     Evidence: On 2b83f512a, separate wall-time re-gates exist in:
      - Initial uncertainty: mod.rs:3828–3837, called at :7307
      - Sized and resized fee adjustment: :4580–4597, called at :7399 and :7491
      - Logs and entry-skip evidence: :3867–3892, used at :4252
      - Submit-linked evidence: :6371–6410

     Issue: A single end-to-end test stops at the first failure. Fixing only that point could make it
     green while later sizing, logging, or evidence still uses wall time. The submit evidence path can
     abort an otherwise valid order.

     Required correction: Require independent mutation differentials for initial pricing, both sizing
     passes, log/skip evidence, and submit-linked evidence. Each mutation back to
     LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms) must fail.

  4. High — The same-head execution plan still instructs the rejected maker design.

     Evidence: The amended spec says the selected quote does not own maker evaluation time (design.md:67–
     69). The implementation plan still says “Maker consumes the selected reference quote receive
     stamp” (plans/2026-07-11-rv-gate-clock-domain.md:78–82).

     Issue: An implementing session following the plan will reproduce the defect the amendment is meant to
     remove.

     Required correction: Update the plan before implementation. Require a route-level differential where:

     quote_receive < snapshot_receive <= explicit_evaluation_receive

     That test fails on 2b83f512a, which currently uses the quote timestamp.

  5. Medium — “Historical snapshot” is broader than the actual cutoff contract.

     Evidence: Computation samples are cutoff-filtered, but source_status sees the full buffer
     (realized_volatility.rs:839–916), rejection diagnostics are present-state fields (:426–432, :861–
     897), and older accepted history is pruned (:501–508).

     Issue: Fixing the watermark does not make the whole snapshot historically reproducible.

     Required correction: Explicitly narrow the promise to “numeric computation and receive watermark
     within retained accepted history.” Otherwise status, diagnostics, and retention must also become
     cutoff-consistent.

  ### Differential verdict

  - Historical newer-than-cutoff test: will fail 2b83f512a, but it must call
    RealizedVolEngine::snapshot_at directly; the runtime does not publish older cutoffs.

  - Maker ordering test: will fail 2b83f512a.
  - Entry near-stale test: will fail 2b83f512a, but must be split as described above.
  - Exit and selection production timestamp paths appeared consistent; no additional hard finding there.

  One scope question remains: maker fair-value initiation is currently unwired in production and used only
  through tests/library routes. The design should describe this as an interface contract unless production
  wiring is intentionally included.

  No files were modified and no tests were run.

     Evidence: The design says evidence records the evaluation’s snapshot without re-gating (.worktrees/
     pr1361/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-11-rv-gate-clock-domain-design.md:100), but the plan only says
     to thread timestamps. Current EntryEvaluation stores no admitted RV snapshot/receipt (.worktrees/
     pr1361/src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/entry_decision.rs:154), while evidence rereads current

     pr1361/src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/mod.rs:6467).

     Issue: An implementation could re-query the latest snapshot using the correct timestamp and still
     record a different snapshot from the one admitted.

     Required correction: Define a typed admitted-RV receipt owned by EntryEvaluation/
     EntrySubmissionDecision, containing the snapshot identity and evidence fields. Logs and submit
     evidence consume that receipt and cannot query current RV state. Add a test that changes latest RV
     state after evaluation and proves evidence still records the admitted snapshot.

     Evidence: The plan requires a full local Rust suite and local clippy (.worktrees/pr1361/docs/

     Issue: The implementer cannot execute the required Rust RED tests through any permitted path.

     Required correction: Choose one compliant evidence route:
      - One scoped remote RED run containing all tests before production changes, followed by
        implementation; or

      - Drop mandatory pre-change execution and use post-implementation mutation proof, since TDD is not
        mandatory.

     Replace local full suite/clippy with cheap local gates plus exact-head remote Rust verification. The
     implementation session should publish a draft SHA and detach; reviewer/orchestrator owns ready-state
     CI, external review, approval, and queueing.

  4. Medium — Cross-source aggregation contributors remain ambiguous.

     Evidence: The design precisely tracks grid-selected observations, but does not define whether sources
     removed by trimmed mean or quantile selection contribute to the final watermark. Current aggregation
     can exclude values numerically while the watermark uses every ready source.

     Required correction: Define whether the watermark covers numeric aggregate contributors or all
     sources affecting readiness/dispersion. Add a trimmed/quantile multi-source differential proving the
     chosen rule.

Design review — #1354 timestamp-ownership amendment @ dd1c5c4c9

- Issue: The amended rule names the set but not the element: it doesn't pin whether the watermark is the max receive timestamp over that set or the receive timestamp of the newest-event sample in it. These differ only if receive time regresses across successive accepted events within one source. reject_observation enforces event monotonicity and same-event receive monotonicity, but nothing rejects cross-event receive regression; today's single-threaded arrival-order ingestion makes the deque receive-monotone in practice, so this is structural, not live-reachable. If a future implementation picks "newest-event sample's receive" and ingestion ever reorders, a snapshot could carry information received after an evaluation's stamp while passing the future-dated check — the exact class #1354 exists to kill.
- Required correction: One sentence in the design pinning the watermark as the maximum receive timestamp over the contributing set, plus an engine unit differential: per-source samples with ascending event times and a deliberately larger receive time on

F2 — Low / undisclosed behavior consequence
- Evidence: Call chain try_submit_entry_order_for_receive → entry_strategy_input_evidence_snapshot_at(now_ms, …)? at src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/mod.rs:7112-7118, whose RV re-gate at line 6407 (LocalReceiveMs::new(now_ms)) bails with "entry strategy input evidence requires realized volatility."
- Issue: Today the submit-evidence helper can veto an already-admitted submit: the decision gates RV at the trigger's receive stamp, then the evidence helper re-gates at strategy-now after order build; if the gate flips in that gap (staleness boundary, or a snapshot refresh moving the watermark), the ? aborts the submission. The amendment's fix (evidence uses the evaluation stamp) removes this last-instant veto, so some entries that abort today will submit after the fix. That is the correct behavior — evidence must describe the decision, not re-decide it — but the Behavioral Blast Radius section doesn't name it; it only names pricing-admission expansion.
- Required correction: Add one blast-radius line ("submit-linked evidence can no longer veto an admitted submission; it records the decision's own gate result") and a discriminating test: RV accepted at the trigger stamp but stale at strategy-now at the submit instant → on 2b83f512a the submit errors out; post-fix it submits and the evidence carries the decision's realized-vol value.

No Blocker/High/Medium findings. Every other invariant I checked holds against the exact-head source.

Answers to the seven questions

1. Missing RV consumers. None found beyond the three disclosed. I swept src/ for every path touching classify_rv_gate, current_realized_vol*, ready_realized_vol, and latest_realized_vol_snapshot: consumption is confined to bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing, bolt_v3_taker_pricing, bolt_v3_maker_runtime_quote, and the taker strategy. The production now_ms re-gates are exactly the amendment's list: uncertainty band (mod.rs:3836, reached from the decision path at 7307 — one evaluation, two stamps), log fields (mod.rs:3879, 3891), and submit evidence (mod.rs:6407). All other fabricating wrappers are #[cfg(test)]. Two nuances the implementation must not miss: (a) entry_evaluation_log_fields_at also feeds durable entry-skip evidence via record_entry_skip_once (mod.rs:4252) — the signature-closure approach fixes both consumers at once, and the skip dedupe key is safe (it reads the evaluation's blocked-by lists, not the re-gated fields); (b) realized_volatility_evidence_fields (mod.rs:6115) reads the raw snapshot with no gate — descriptive evidence only, correctly outside the rule.

2. Callers lacking a genuine receive stamp. Non(ts_init, 7700-7704), signal quote (received timestamp, 1561-1565), selection update (strategy-clock boundary conversion, 977-981); the fourth is #[cfg(test)]. Maker route callers all hold quote_set.now_ms and the triggering quote context, so an explicit evaluation stamp is supplyable. One boundary case: after_signal_quote_observed (mod.rs:1553-1554) falls back to the strategy clock when a quote carries no receive metadata — every QuoteTick path sets Some(ts_init) (mod.rs:1625), and the fallback is a trigger-boundary conversion the design explicitly permits, but see question Q3 below.

3. Historical-snapshot watermark vs. contributing set. The rule is correctly derived, with F1's precision caveat. The value computation filters exactly on sample.event_ts_ms <= as_of_ms (bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:842), which is the same set the amendment names; the surface watermark aggregates only quorum-ready sources (lines 581-584), matching the original design. Pre-window samples legitimately belong in the set (they seed the grid via grid_prices, lines 926-936). Also verified: both live refresh paths (refresh_surface_at, publish_surface_after_routed_observation) pass as_of = latest_accepted_event_ts, under which the per-source back sample always passes the filter — so the fix changes no live-path watermark; it is reachable only via historical as_of, exactly as the design frames it.

4. Maker evaluation-time ownership. Correctly assigned. Head borrows selected_quote.received_ts_ms() (bolt_v3_maker_runtime_quote.rs:167) — that timestamp describes an input, and the amendment reassigns ownership to a caller-supplied stamp, which the invariant list already required ("the pricing evaluation carries a receive-domain timestamp"). Note the strategy is registered but this route isn't wired), so the fix is contract-closing for maker bring-up rather than a live behavior change.

5. Undisclosed behavior change / scope expansion. One consequence gap (F2). Otherwise clean: no tolerance window, cap, sampling rule, or venue exception appears anywhere in the amendment; the 1 MiB recovery ceiling is unchanged and pinned by the boundary tests added in 2b83f512a; the follow-on section matches the approved four-PR program exactly (#1275-13 before soak, #883 then #763 later); historical-watermark fix has zero live-path effect; the maker fix widens admission only in the direction its verification bullet discloses.

6. Differentials red on 2b83f512a. All three new bullets fail on the original commit and prove the corrected behavior:
- Near-stale entry: set watermark w, trigger receive r with r − w ≤ max_age, strategy-now n with n − w > max_age. On head, fair-value gates at r (accepted) but the band gates at n (mod.rs:7307 → 3836) → evaluation blocked UncertaintyBandUnavailable; log/evidence realized-vol fields go None while the decision had Some. Post-fix both use r → red then green. The future-dated direction can't produce divergence (fair value at r rejects first, line 7221), so staleness is the right axis — worth stating in the test plan.
- Historical surface: engine-level snapshot_at(as_of) with a buffered accepted sample newer than as_of — head returns that sample's receive as the watermark (samples.back(), line 538-542) → red; filtered derivation → green.head rejects future-dated through the borrowed stamp (line 167) → red; explicit stamp → green. The existing test maker_pricing_does_not_compare_independent_venue_clocks (tests/bolt_v3_maker_runtime_quote.rs:240) stays green across the change, so no pinned test must be weakened.

7. Contradictions with original #1354 invariants. None. The amendment replaces the one conflicting sentence (maker borrowing the quote stamp) rather than layering on it, and original invariant 3 already said "latest accepted contributing observation" — the head implementation violates that wording for historical queries, so the amendment brings the plan into line with the original invariant rather than amending it. Fail-closed states are preserved end-to-end in classify_rv_gate (bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:449-469), rejected observations still can't advance either watermark (observe returns before push, bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:484-491), and "no production trigger constructs MissingEvaluationEventTime" is true at head (all four production trigger constructors pass Some).

Questions / observations (non-blocking)

- Q1 — same defect class in the reference/spot staleness gate. current_reference_pricing_event_ms (mod.rs:3620-3636) takes the max of the signal venue's and reference venue's event clocks, and reference_current_price_stale_at (bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs:319-332, used at 416-435) compares that against spot/reference venue event timestamps —skew, and the gate fails closed (it can only spuriously block, not admit), so the exposure is far smaller than the RV defect — but it is the same class. Is it deliberately out of #1354 and tracked anywhere?
- Q2 — where does the archived-corpus replay live? The contract's first bullet ("archived inputs reproduce every current classifier result") appears to be an offline recipe against the session archive; the in-repo tests added at 2b83f512a are synthetic. If data governance keeps the corpus out of the repo, the design could name the replay recipe/artifact so the amendment's re-run is reproducible evidence rather than a prose claim.
- Q3 — make the signal-quote clock fallback an acknowledged boundary conversion. received_ts_ms.unwrap_or_else(strategy clock) at mod.rs:1553-1554 is silent. It's consistent with the design's local-trigger rule and unreachable from QuoteTick paths today, but a sentence in the design blessing (or forbidding) it would keep "no silent replacement" airtight.

Primary finding

Severity: Medium
Evidence: current_reference_pricing_event_ms, src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/mod.rs:3623-3636 (max over three VenueEventMs values); reference_current_price_stale_at, src/bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs:319-332; its two call sites src/bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs:416-423 (spot arm) and :425-435 (reference arm); block-reason emission :439-441 (SpotPriceMissing) and :460-462 (ReferenceCurrentPriceStale); live config config/strategies/binary_oracle_*.toml [reference_current_price] max_source_age_ms = 2000 mapped at mod.rs:583-586.
Issue: The evaluation coordinate is the maximum event timestamp across the OKX signal spot (ETH-USDT.OKX exchange clock) and the selected reference quote (Chainlink WS primary / polyresearch backup provider clocks) — independently disciplined physical clocks with no upstream normalization. Each input's own event timestamp is then subtracted from that cross-venue maximum. The spot arm is the purest case: the spot term can never exceed the max it belongs to, so stale_pricing_spot can only fire through the reference clock outrunning the spot clock — the check's entire discriminating power is a cross-venue event comparison. The same ordering-domain violation #1354 fixed for RV admission.
Reachability: Requires cross-clock lead plus genuine input age to exceed 2,000 ms. The #1354 incident measured 1–347 ms skew (median 20 ms) — but between a different clock pair (execution-venue book vs. spot surface), so it bounds nothing here; OKX↔Chainlink skew is unmeasured (uncertainty, stated below). Healthy operation: unreachable. Provider clock disturbance or degraded oracle rounds stamping observations seconds off wall-clock: plausibly reachable, i.e., exactly incident conditions.
Behavioral consequence: Entry-only (the coordinate flows solely into TakerPricingRequest.reference_gate_event_ms, mod.rs:3767/3798; exits and forced-flat use separate mechanisms). On rejection: pricing_blocked_by gains ReferenceCurrentPriceStale — or SpotPriceMissing, a mislabel (staleness reported as absence) — → ENTRY_BLOCK_REASON_ENTRY_PRICING_BLOCKED, no order. Evidence: EntrySkipDedupeKey includes the blocked-by set (mod.rs:4254-4271) behind a single-slot dedupe (last_recorded_entry_skip), so reason-sets alternating across interleaved OKX/Chainlink ticks defeat dedupe entirely — the same oscillation-defeats-single-slot pattern as the #1354 exit flood and the PR #1356 finding, now on the entry-skip stream. No later layer masks a rejection; upstream, the spot arm is dormant until the first signal quote (lead_quality_policy_applied, taker_pricing.rs:180/223), and in pre-signal failover mode the reference itself owns the pricing spot (taker_pricing.rs:136-141).
Recommended scope ruling: B — confirmed defect, separate issue/branch/PR. #1354 is coherent alone: the two gates live in independent request fields (realized_vol_gate_receive_ms vs reference_gate_event_ms, taker_pricing.rs:47-48); Task 4's required-LocalReceiveMs change neither reads nor writes the reference field; and the follow-up becomes smaller after #1354 because the required evaluation receive stamp it threads to both call sites is precisely the coordinate the fixed reference gate needs — the follow-up can consume it and delete reference_gate_event_ms. Ruling C is refuted by that same fact; ruling A is refuted by the venue identities in live config.
Required correction (in the follow-up, not #1354): Compare evaluation receive time against input receive time; keep event timestamps diagnostic. Two state-retention gaps must close for that: FairValuePricingState keeps only last_reference_observed_ts_ms (fair_value_pricing.rs:115-117) and ActiveMarketState only observed (runtime_state.rs:372-373), although the receive value reaches both boundaries (mod.rs:1951; ReferenceQuote.received_ts_ms, bolt_v3_reference_price.rs:119-120). Fix the SpotPriceMissing mislabel in the same issue. Discriminating differential below.

Secondary findings (same class, for the follow-up census)

Severity: Low — Evidence: reference_quote_outside_live_window, mod.rs:603-615 and its selector twin bolt_v3_reference_price.rs:720, via NtStrategyClockMs::saturating_duration_since_venue_event (bolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs:66) — Issue: source-health staleness subtracts a provider event timestamp from the local strategy clock (third cross-domain instance; drives per-source Stale status and failover churn) — Reachability/consequence: same >2,000 ms skew bar; failover flapping, fail-closed — Ruling: fold into the follow-up census — Correction: receive-domain age for health, event time diagnostic.

Severity: Low — Evidence: forced-flat StaleReference, src/bolt_v3_feed_health.rs:38 (now_ms − last_reference_ts_ms, where last_reference_ts_ms is venue event time from runtime_state.rs:373) — Issue: local-clock-minus-venue-event, fourth instance; fires exits flat on apparenappears only in feed_health.

The eight questions

1. Producers of the three combined values. (1) selected_pricing_spot().observed_ts_ms — OKX exchange clock via QuoteTick.ts_event (signal_quote_from_tick, mod.rs:1619-1626), receive = Some(ts_init), retained; in pre-signal failover the reference quote itself becomes the spot (taker_pricing.rs:136-141), then this input is on the reference provider's clock. (2) last_reference_current_price_ts_ms — selected ReferenceQuote.observed_ts_ms() (Chainlink WS primary / polyresearch backup provider clocks), ingested with receive time by observe_reference_price_update (mod.rs:1749-1767), receive passed to pricing (mod.rs:1946-1952) but not retained in state. (3) active.last_reference_ts_ms — the same selected quote's observed_ts_ms stored in active state (runtime_state.rs:372-373), receive likewise dropped. Inputs 2 and 3 are the same upstream value in two stores and normally identical.

2. Live callers/consumers of reference_current_price_stale_at. It is private to TakerPricingState with exactly two call sites, both inside entry_pricing_inputs_at (spot arm :416-423, reference arm :425-435); entry_pricing_at routes through the same gate (taker_pricing.rs:485-492). Strategy-side reach: production entry evaluation only (current_entry_pricing_inputs_for_receive_at / current_fair_probability_up_for_receive_at, mod.rs:3761/3792, book-delta trcall sites.

3. Direct ordering or normalized upstream? Direct. Raw ts_event/provider-stamped observed_ts_ms flow unmodified into the max and the subtraction; there is no offset estimation or domain conversion anywhere on the path. Confirmed cross-venue ordering on a production entry path.

4. Exact consequence on rejection. Covered in the primary finding: blocked entry (ENTRY_BLOCK_REASON_ENTRY_PRICING_BLOCKED), reason ReferenceCurrentPriceStale or mislabeled SpotPriceMissing, WARN on reason-set change, durable entry-skip records whose single-slot dedupe is defeated by alternation; nothing downstream masks it.

5. Limits vs skew. Configured window: 2,000 ms (all six live strategy TOMLs). Incident-measured skew: 1–347 ms, wrong clock pair to bound this gate. Behavior flips exactly when cross-clock lead + genuine input age exceeds 2,000 ms (with fresh inputs: lead > 2,000 ms). Steady-state exchange/oracle clocks sit orders of magnitude below that; degraded oracle rounds plausibly cross it. Per the brief: the large window limits reachability, not validity — the comparison is semantically invalid regardless.

6. Receive availability. Every producer carries trustworthy local receive time at source (OKX ts_init; ReferenceQuote.received_ts_ms required field for both primary and backup — both flow through the same to_reference_quote ingestion). Nothing is structurally missing at source; the two state stores drop it (named above). The evaluation side already has its receive stamp via #1354's book-trigger threading.

7. Discriminating differential. At TakerPricingState level, hold receive constant, move only event clocks: observe signal quote (OKX, observed=11_500, received=5_000) and reference (chainlink, observed=9_000, received=5_000); evaluate with reference_gate_event_ms = max = 11_500, max_age = 2_000 → current code emits ReferenceCurrentPriceStale (age 2,500). Control arm: signal observed=10_500 → accepted (age 1,500). Same receive times in both arms, so yes — current code changes the stale result on event-clock skew alone, which is the defect proof; under the receive-domain rule both arms accept. This test belongs to the follow-up PR, not #1354, per the Task 0 ruling structure.

8. Ruling: B. Confirmed defect; own issue, branch, design, and PR. #1354 remains coherent alone for the reasons in the primary finding — independent request fields, independent state, and the follow-up consumes what #1354 builds rather than fighting it.

Assessment of the proposed rule

Sound as written, and it matches the #1354 doctrine exactly: receive-vs-receive comparison, event timestamps demoted to diagnostics, no tolerance window or venue exception, separate PR (atomicity is not required — demonstrated above). Four additions so the follow-up closes the class rather than the instance: retain input receive times in the two state stores; include the two adjacent local-vs-venue-event comparisons (selector live-window, forced-flat StaleReference) in the same census; fix the SpotPriceMissing mislabel so evidence names staleness truthfully; sequence after #1354 merges so the gate consumes the already-threaded evaluation stamp and reference_gate_event_ms can be deleted rather than migrated.

Uncertainty, stated explicitly

OKX↔Chainlink/polyresearch clock skew is unmeasured — the 1–347 ms incident figure is a different clock pair and bounds nothing here. All analysis is static reading at f572791db; I executed no code and no replay. The polyresearch backup adapter's timestamp stamping was not inspected below the ReferencePriceUpdate boundary. The forced-flat threshold's live TOML value was not chased down. None of these change the ruling; the first one only widens the reachability error bars in both directions.

Answers to review questions

1. Upstream behavior

Verified facts

• Pin range 9e71b2b… → afc014a… is three commits only:
  • fa3391d — functional: preserve SBE adapter init timestamps
  • 7c8fc13 — rustfmt frozen base fixture include
  • afc014a — merge/rustfmt repair
• Diff touches only three NT files: spot/data.rs, spot/websocket/streams/parse.rs, spot/http/parse.rs (http change is rustfmt of include_bytes! only).
• At SBE handler boundary (BinanceSpotDataClient::handle_ws_message):

let ts_init = clock.get_time_ns();
// trades / BBO / depth snapshot / depth diff all receive that same ts_init

• Parsers: parse_trades_event, parse_bbo_event, parse_depth_snapshot, parse_depth_diff take ts_init and stop copying ts_event into init.
• Multi-item trades: one message-level ts_init applied to every trade; per-trade ts_event remains transact time.
• Depth: every inner delta and aggregate (OrderBookDeltas::new takes last delta’s ts_init) use the supplied init stamp.
• Buffered depth: stamps at parse time before buffer; HTTP snapshot resync takes a new clock.get_time_ns() at emit — correct for that path.
• ts_event remains Binance event/transact micros converted via UnixNanos::from_micros.
• NT PR #1 head is exactly afc014a…; Linux Python build/pre-commit jobs reported success on run 29165821333.

Unrelated / risk in pin

• No unrelated feature work in the functional commit. Rustfmt-only noise is present and low risk.
• Spot JSON path already took caller ts_init (pre-existing). Futures client also uses clock-supplied init. This pin’s bug was SBE ts_event/ts_event duplication.

Inference

• #1354 quote consumers care about BBO/QuoteTick; trades/depth correctness is extra hygiene in the same fix class.

GPT: REQUEST CHANGES

  ## Substantive findings

  ### 1. Severity: High — Behavioral dependency proof covers only BBO parsing

  - Evidence: tests/binance_sbe_quote_timestamps.rs:56

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/a9b4fc62ea5e7f6fbf929de90ef820586ca5f47f/tests/binance_sbe_quote_timestamps.rs#L56-L75)

  - Issue: The Bolt test calls parse_bbo_event directly. It does not behaviorally cover multi-trade output, depth snapshot/diff
    inner deltas, aggregate wrappers, buffered replay, or BinanceSpotDataClient::handle_ws_message capturing and supplying the clock
    timestamp.

  - Consequence: It can pass while the production handler supplies ts_event as ts_init, or while trade/depth timestamp propagation
    regresses. NT’s own tests cover these parsers and one BBO handler case, but dependency unit tests are not run by Bolt’s Cargo
    suite.

  - Required correction: Extend Bolt-side behavioral proof across every public relevant parser output. Add enforceable handler-
    boundary proof, or explicitly bind that private-handler proof to the reviewed NT revision with a corresponding source contract.

  - Scope ruling: PR #1367.

  ### 2. Severity: High — Governance does not make the behavioral harness semantically mandatory

  - Evidence:
      - Cargo.toml:53 (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/a9b4fc62ea5e7f6fbf929de90ef820586ca5f47f/Cargo.toml#L53-L55)
      - scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py:49

        (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/a9b4fc62ea5e7f6fbf929de90ef820586ca5f47f/scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L49-L68)

      - scripts/run_fences.py:137
        (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/a9b4fc62ea5e7f6fbf929de90ef820586ca5f47f/scripts/run_fences.py#L137-L149)

  - Issue: The boundary verifier never requires binance_sbe_quote_timestamps, its registration, or its assertions. The global
    harness-count check could be satisfied by an unrelated replacement harness.

  - Consequence: The required behavioral proof can silently disappear while the textual lineage and pin census remain green.
  - Required correction: Fail the verifier when the exact test target/file is absent, unregistered, or no longer contains the
    required behavioral cases. Add negative fixtures for each failure mode.

  - Scope ruling: PR #1367.

  ## 1. Upstream behavior

  Verified facts from NT range 9e71b2b1305a66945ba07f0aba2d1eb63208263d...afc014a55b51463641cc19c68bffe25cdac6588a:

  - Three commits:
      - fa3391d9 — timestamp fix.
      - 7c8fc131 — rustfmt-only fixture formatting.
      - afc014a5 — merge commit.

  - Only three dependency files changed:
      - crates/adapters/binance/src/spot/data.rs
      - crates/adapters/binance/src/spot/websocket/streams/parse.rs
      - formatting-only crates/adapters/binance/src/spot/http/parse.rs

  - data.rs:341

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/nautilus_trader/blob/afc014a55b51463641cc19c68bffe25cdac6588a/crates/adapters/binance/src/spot/data.rs#L341-L386)
    captures clock.get_time_ns() once before message dispatch and passes that same value to trades, BBO, depth snapshots, and depth
    diffs.

  - Parsers preserve Binance timestamps as ts_event and use the supplied value as ts_init.
  - Every trade in a multi-trade message receives the shared ts_init.
  - Snapshot/diff aggregate wrappers and every inner delta receive the shared timestamp.
  - Buffered depth updates retain their already-parsed OrderBookDeltas; replay does not restamp them.
  - No unrelated risky behavior was found. Existing SBE schema support is unchanged.

  Inference: the reviewed revision implements the intended clock-domain correction.

  Unmeasured: production traffic frequency and runtime clock skew were not measured.

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/a9b4fc62ea5e7f6fbf929de90ef820586ca5f47f/config/root.toml#L42-L51) selects the same
    quote stream as a BTC realized-volatility midpoint source.

  - Strategy quote handling feeds realized volatility before signal handling; both consume the same QuoteTick.
  - The alternate public-JSON Spot handler already captures local clock time at its boundary.

  ## 3. Bolt dependency proof

  - It proves unequal ts_event/ts_init behavior for BBO parser output.
  - It does not prove all relevant parser outputs.
  - It does not prove production-handler clock capture.
  - Yes, it can pass while the production handler supplies the wrong timestamp.

  This is blocking finding 1.

  ## 4. Pin completeness

  Revision afc014a55b51463641cc19c68bffe25cdac6588a is consistent across:

  - Root Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock
  - BVS Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock
  - Runtime contract
  - NT naming/revision ledger
  - Verifier constant
  - Verifier-test fixture

  All root lockfile NT packages use one canonical source; all BVS lockfile NT packages do likewise. No old revision, alternate NT
  source, stale transitive NT package, or root/BVS mismatch was found.

  ## 5. Governance enforcement

  Verified:

  - Binance Spot SBE ownership is registered for RealizedVolatilityObservation and StrategySignalObservation.
  - Exact SHA, handler, decoder, and BBO parser names are textually fenced.

1. Upstream Behavior
Verified facts (from direct inspection of NT fork commits 9e71b2b..afc014a5):

Commit	Description
fa3391d9	fix(binance): preserve SBE adapter init timestamps
7c8fc131	chore(binance): rustfmt frozen base fixture include
afc014a5	chore(binance): merge frozen-base rustfmt repair
Local clock captured once at handler boundary — confirmed at crates/adapters/binance/src/spot/data.rs:340:
let ts_init = clock.get_time_ns();
This precedes the match msg block. clock is the existing &'static AtomicTime parameter — no new clock, no new dependency.
Every emitted data type receives the same ts_init — verified across all four SBE message arms:
- Trades (data.rs:345): parse_trades_event(event, instrument, ts_init) — every TradeTick in the vector gets the same ts_init
- BBO (data.rs:358): parse_bbo_event(event, instrument, ts_init) — QuoteTick.ts_init = ts_init
- DepthSnapshot (data.rs:366): parse_depth_snapshot(event, instrument, ts_init) — both aggregate OrderBookDeltas.ts_init and every inner OrderBookDelta.ts_init use ts_init
- DepthDiff (data.rs:375): parse_depth_diff(event, instrument, ts_init) — same pattern; deltas already have ts_init baked before route_depth_diff buffering, so deferred/buffered processing does not restamp
ts_event remains Binance event timestamp — confirmed in every parser: ts_event = UnixNanos::from_micros(event.event_time_us as u64) (or transact_time_us for trades). Only ts_init changed from ts_event to the caller-supplied local value.
Non-data message arms (ServerShutdown, RawBinary, RawJson, Error, Reconnected) produce no data events with timestamps. The Reconnected arm calls rebuild_full_depth_books which uses the HTTP snapshot path (already clock-correct in the prior revision) — not a WebSocket frame path.

3. Bolt Dependency Proof
Verified facts (from tests/binance_sbe_quote_timestamps.rs):
The test constructs a BestBidAskStreamEvent with event_time_us = 1_700_000_000_000_000 and calls parse_bbo_event with adapter_ts_init = UnixNanos::from(1_800_000_000_000_000_000), then asserts:
- quote.ts_event == UnixNanos::from_micros(event_time_us) — venue time preserved
- quote.ts_init == adapter_ts_init — caller-supplied value used, not event time
- The two are explicitly assert_ne!
This is a behavioral test of the parser output, not a signature/symbol check. It proves the parser uses the ts_init parameter for ts_init and derives ts_event from event time only.
Could it pass while a production handler still supplies the wrong timestamp? In theory yes — the test calls parse_bbo_event directly, not through the handler. If the handler were changed to pass ts_event as the ts_init argument, the parser test would still pass. However, this gap is covered by three complementary mechanisms:
1. The pin locks the exact NT revision where the handler captures clock.get_time_ns()
2. The NT fork's own test (handle_ws_message_uses_clock_timestamp_for_sbe_ts_init) tests the handler boundary end-to-end
3. The runtime contract fence requires BinanceSpotDataClient::handle_ws_message to be documented in section 11.5
This is an honest boundary — Bolt cannot independently test NT's internal handler without reaching into NT internals, but the pin + NT test + fence combination covers it.

  # reads the body first and only opens artifacts if it needs more detail.

  # from a failed retry cannot complete the current dispatch.

    --type worker_done --subject "<short status>" \
    --body "<3-sentence summary: what you did, what you found, what's left>" \
    --task-id task_4ee91d759dd3 --dispatch-id ctx_b83cd87237cb \
    --files-modified "path/a,path/b" \
    --report-path "<optional: path to the full artifact>"

  # BEHAVIOR RULE: send a heartbeat every 5 minutes
  # while actively working on the task. The coordinator uses this to
  # distinguish "still thinking" from "hung / crashed." Skip heartbeats only
  # while blocked inside `check --wait` or `ask` — those calls are
  # themselves liveness signals.
  #
  # Include BOTH taskId and dispatchId in the payload: the coordinator
  # attributes the heartbeat to the specific dispatch context, not just
  # the task, so a straggler heartbeat from a previously-failed dispatch
  # cannot mask a hung retry.

    --type heartbeat --subject "alive" \

  # waiting on a human. Every interactive question goes through `ask` below.

  # The `ask` verb is a thin wrapper: it sends a decision_gate message and
  # blocks on `check --wait` until the coordinator replies, then prints the

  # AskUserQuestion.
  orca orchestration ask --to term_d1d7ea69-8dec-454b-9b30-e313157c53ac \
    --question "<your question>" \
