Design Spec Review — BVS Compile-Compatibility Cache

Reviewer: Claude Code CLI session, model claude-fable-5 (harness-declared ID), reasoning effort xhigh as configured for this session by the operator (the effort setting is operator-configured; the model ID is the verifiable anchor).
Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-12-bvs-compatibility-cache-design.md at commit 98764840a3fb073320682a24502ef97d776a2b81 (worktree HEAD verified to match).
Method: read-only. Every claim below is anchored to a file read at the pinned commit, a live GitHub API read, or the actual failed CI run log. No files edited, no Rust compiled, nothing mutated.

Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES

The core design — exact compatibility-seed keys, no prefix fallback, main-only writes, truthful telemetry, budget-gated with disabled-cache as the honest default — is sound, and its factual claims are unusually accurate (I verified the cache inventory byte-for-byte and replayed the incident from the real run log). But three findings are substantive spec gaps that would force the implementer to make unreviewed contract decisions or produce impossible evidence. They are fixable in the spec without changing the decision.

---
Findings

F1 — MAJOR: The fate of the shared digest step (and the bootstrap namespace for S3 keys) is unspecified

Evidence (verified): In backtester-ci.yml, one step (bvs_cache_inputs, the "Compute BVS cache input hash" step) produces a single digest consumed by both the managed-target cache keys and the S3 nextest/sidecar keys (bvs-nextest-archive-v4-..., bvs-bin-sidecars-v4-...). When the resolver or its TOML changes, that step emits bootstrap-${GITHUB_SHA} — so today, S3 keys also get a commit-SHA identity in that case. On top of that, the current hygiene verifier mandates both things the design bans: backtester_managed_target_cache_errors in scripts/verify_ci_workflow_hygiene.py requires every BVS cache key to use that one shared digest step, and requires the bootstrap-SHA branch to exist in backtester-ci.yml ("backtester cache key digest must use exact-head namespace when CI input-set bootstrap changes").

Issue: The spec says the verifier "must fail if it finds bootstrap-${GITHUB_SHA}, any other commit-SHA namespace" — with ambiguous scope — and says S3 keys "continue to use the full-source backtester_cache digest," which misdescribes today's behavior (they don't, in the bootstrap case). Splitting the digest step is mechanically forced (target keys need the new seed; S3 keys need the full-source digest), but the spec never states what the S3 keys do when the resolver changes once the bootstrap branch is gone. An implementer keeping bootstrap for S3 keys and a hygiene rule banning all commit-SHA namespaces are mutually contradictory.

Required correction: Specify explicitly: (a) two separate digest steps with per-family key rules in the hygiene contract; (b) the S3 key/metadata identity in the resolver-change case (the safe answer: always the head-resolver backtester_cache digest, bootstrap namespace eliminated everywhere, with a sentence on why save/restore-at-same-head keeps the metadata contract intact); (c) an explicit note that two existing verifier rules are being inverted/split, so the mutation tests target the right invariants.

F2 — MAJOR: The budget gate measures a snapshot; steady-state coexistence with the root cache family is unmodeled

Evidence (measured live during this review): The repository's five current cache entries are all root-lane managed-target-v1-Linux-ARM64-test-archive-test-<digest> caches, ~1.82 GiB each, five distinct digests accessed within roughly 36 hours — the root family alone churns near the entire 10 GiB budget. The 6,419,854,292-byte BVS v3 cache that was restored at 08:11 UTC today is already gone from the inventory (LRU-evicted).

Issue: The rollout gate (spec steps 1–4) accepts on pre-save headroom plus one post-save snapshot. It does not account for: (a) root-family churn continuously refilling the budget; (b) GitHub's LRU eviction, which would repeatedly evict an accepted ~6.4 GiB BVS cache and cause it to be re-saved on each subsequent main push — permanent upload/storage thrash that a snapshot-passing rollout would misreport as success; (c) seed transitions (every NT-pin/lockfile change) leaving the prior seed's entries alive until eviction/expiry, so the family transiently holds two seeds per lane, which the criterion-2 projection doesn't count.

Required correction: Make the gate a steady-state projection — BVS family (both lanes) + 2× seed-transition transient + the measured root-family churn window must fit under 10 GiB — and add a post-rollout stability criterion (e.g., no eviction-driven BVS re-save across N main pushes, verified from the existing storage-audit evidence). Note this strengthens, not reverses, the spec's own stated default: on current evidence the disabled-cache fallback becomes even more clearly the outcome.

F3 — MEDIUM: The verification matrix demands evidence that cannot exist in the spec's own default outcome

Evidence (verified in spec text): The "Rust and workflow behavior" row requires "a later same-seed source-only head gets an exact hit" as mandatory remote evidence, while the budget section states "the default outcome is the disabled-cache fallback." Under the fallback there are no BVS target-cache steps, so an exact hit can never be observed. The matrix has a "Fallback" row but never scopes the enabled-lane rows as conditional.

Issue: Read literally, the implementation cannot satisfy its own mandatory evidence table in the default path — and under AGENTS.md, every requirement must map to producible evidence.

Required correction: Split the matrix into "enabled-lane evidence" and "fallback evidence" sets, each binding only for the branch actually selected by the budget gate, with the gate's own measurement as the recorded selector.

F4 — LOW: The 32 GiB authority citation names only half of the governing policy

Evidence (verified): The BVS lanes run rust_verification.py run --repo crates/backtesting-vertical-slice (the bte-clippy/bte-test recipes), so the operative policy file for the incident lane is crates/backtesting-vertical-slice/ci/rust-verification.toml (soft_limit_bytes = 32212254720 at its [cache] section). The spec's telemetry section and read-only authority list cite only root ci/rust-verification.toml (same value). Relatedly, the spec's description of backtester_cache as including "build policy" holds only for the BVS-local policy file — the root one is not a member of that set.

Required correction: Cite both files as 32 GiB authorities and require the "32 GiB limit retained" test to pin both values, so drift in the file that actually governs the lane can't slip past a root-only assertion.

F5 — LOW: "Twice on the same head" is not anchored, and an interleaved success is unexplained

Evidence (verified): At PR #1367's current head ff174ca18 there is exactly one Backtester CI run (29185376240, single attempt). I verified that run's failure mode end-to-end from its log: exact miss → prefix restore of managed-target-bvs-v3-Linux-ARM64-test-bootstrap-9f3b13f4c... (6,419,854,292 bytes — a per-commit bootstrap cache saved from main, which is a nice confirmation that the bootstrap namespace itself was the poison source) → build → disk_pressure refusal at the binary-sidecar preflight with free_bytes: 79150555136 and reclaimable_bytes: 0, exactly as the spec describes. The earlier head a9b4fc62e has two Backtester failures and one success between them (runs 29177797488 failure, 29180025916 success, 29180923906 failure); I did not verify those two failures' mode individually.

Issue: The incident section overstates reproducibility precision — a reviewer replaying "twice on the same head" at the PR's current head finds one occurrence, and the same-head success undermines the narrative if left unaddressed.

Required correction: Pin the incident claims to exact run IDs and head SHAs, and either explain the interleaved success (duplicate-event run with skipped jobs, or S3 archive hit bypassing the build) or soften to "repeatedly across PR #1367 heads."

F6 — LOW: Runner-image residual risk is real and unacknowledged

Evidence (verified): The lane's runner comes from the GitHub variable CI_RUNNER_MANAGED_HEAVY; ci/github-actions-runners.toml registers only the variable name. Key separators cover runner.os/runner.arch only.

Issue: A runner-image change (glibc, system libraries) keeps both the seed and the key constant while potentially invalidating compiled-artifact compatibility. The seed's claim to include "every checked-in surface" is literally true — but this not-checked-in surface survives from v3 into v4 unmentioned, and the spec's framing invites the reader to believe compatibility is fully keyed.

Required correction: One paragraph acknowledging the residual, and the accepted rationale (worst case is a failed build caught by CI, not a silently wrong artifact — or add an image identifier to the key if one is reliably available).

F7 — INFO: State the resolver-policy asymmetry so it isn't "fixed" by the implementer

FORBIDDEN_BACKTESTER_CACHE_TARGETS in scripts/ci_input_sets.py hard-forbids backtester_cache from covering the workflow file, the resolver, its TOML, and the setup action — three of which the new seed requires as members. Set-scoped, so not a contradiction, but the spec's self-test section should say the seed's membership policy is intentionally inverse on those paths.

---
Verified facts vs. inference vs. unmeasured

Verified facts (all anchors personally re-read/re-run at HEAD or fetched live this session): all 30 files the spec names exist at the pinned commit; the v3 keys and broad restore-keys prefixes are exactly at the cited sites in all three workflows; saves are already main-only and flaky lanes already restore-only (the writer-authority section retains current behavior — accurate, though the spec presents it as new design); digest_input_set does hash ordered pathspec declarations plus tracked bytes, so the deterministic-bootstrap claim holds; both 32 GiB values and the 10 GiB cache-listed-bytes warning threshold are exact; the live inventory matches the spec byte-for-byte (5 entries, 9,118,987,793 total, 1,618,430,447 headroom, no surviving BVS entry); the 6,419,854,292-byte prior cache is confirmed from the run log; today's refusal payload contains no measured-target-size field and hardcodes reclaimable_bytes: 0, so the telemetry redesign fixes a real, confirmed gap; the hash <set_name> CLI shape is correct; the slice is CI-only, bundles no clock-domain code, and unblocks #1367 in both outcomes (a fallback cold build starts from an empty target, so the 32 GiB refusal can't re-trigger from restored payload). No conflict with AGENTS.md found.

Inference (reasonable, not directly measured): the 32 GiB overage being caused by the mixture of two dependency graphs — well-supported by the 6.4 GiB compressed restore plus a full new NT graph compile, and the spec itself correctly refuses to claim byte apportionment.

Unmeasured (spec should keep treating as such): the clippy-lane cache size (spec's 6.42 GiB proxy is reasonable but per-lane sizes are unknown until first save); whether a clean cold-build target under the new NT pin stays below 32 GiB (correctly covered by the matrix's "cold miss succeeds" requirement).

---
Bottom line: REQUEST CHANGES on F1–F3 (F4–F7 should be folded into the same revision). The design's decisions are right and its evidence discipline is genuinely good — the incident narrative survived a full adversarial replay against the real run log, which is rare. Resolve the three gaps in the spec text and it is fit to gate a plan. Per the spec's own status line, no plan or implementation work should start until these findings are resolved and re-reviewed.

FINAL SPEC RE-REVIEW — BVS S3 Compiler Cache and Target-Cache Removal Design

Reviewer: Claude Code CLI session, model claude-fable-5 (harness-declared ID), reasoning effort xhigh as configured for this session by the operator.
Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-12-bvs-compatibility-cache-design.md at commit bea2eef91bc97b1d927557ea08912232cedbfbe1 (verified: worktree HEAD matches; single-file docs commit, 263 insertions / 175 deletions over the R1 spec).
Method: full re-review (not a delta review), read-only. Every claim anchored to files read at the pinned commit or live GitHub API reads. Nothing edited, compiled, pushed, or mutated.

Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES — narrow

All seven prior findings are resolved, the pivot is architecturally right, and every infrastructure claim I tested against the repo and live CI checked out. What remains is three LOW findings — spec-text corrections with unambiguous fixes, none of which changes the design decision, the authority model, or the evidence architecture. Under this repo's own gate ("every finding is resolved before an implementation plan exists"), the consistent verdict is REQUEST CHANGES; expect the next round to be short and final.

---
Prior findings F1–F7: all RESOLVED

F1 (shared digest step / bootstrap fate for S3 keys) — RESOLVED. The revision eliminates the bootstrap-SHA branch from all BVS identity, makes the single digest step's ownership explicit (S3 artifacts only), specifies head-resolver consistency for restore key, save key, and metadata within one run, and explicitly enumerates the two hygiene-verifier rules being inverted — which match the actual rules in backtester_managed_target_cache_errors (the shared-bvs_cache_inputs requirement and the mandatory bootstrap-namespace requirement) that I verified in scripts/verify_ci_workflow_hygiene.py.

F2 (snapshot budget gate vs steady-state churn) — RESOLVED by dissolution: the BVS Actions target-cache budget is now exactly zero entries/bytes/saves, the root-family churn and eviction dynamics are documented as the reason, and a four-point API-backed inventory (before, after main, after two source-only heads) guards against reappearance and re-save loops. Root allocation is correctly declared separate scope. (A residual precision defect in this section is new finding N1 below.)

F3 (matrix conditionality) — RESOLVED. The matrix is now three sections — always-required static, enabled-sccache, fail-open/outage — with an explicit non-substitution paragraph in both directions. The sequencing secthat performance qualification is necessarily post-rollout because only trusted main writes.

F4 (32 GiB authority) — RESOLVED. Both policy files are named with the exact value, the BVS-local file is correctly identified as governing the incident lane, and static tests must pin both. Verified: soft_limit_bytes = 32212254720 present in both ci/rust-verification.toml and crates/backtesting-vertical-slice/ci/rust-verification.toml.
 the spec says — bvs-clippy: skipped, bvs-test archive: skipped, backtester-gate-iteration: success. The interleaved "success" is genuinely an iteration-policy run and not a heavy-lane counterexample. The narrative is also appropriately hedged ("does not prove a byte-by-byte split... makes no such claim").

F6 (runner-image residual) — RESOLVED. The new section is honest: variable name checked in, no stable image digest available, expected failure mode is a loud CI failure followed by clean rebuild, and a repeated image-specific failure requires a source-backed design rather than an invented key.

F7 (resolver-policy asymmetry) — RESOLVED. The "Intentional input-set asymmetry" section accurately describes the FORBIDDEN_BACKTESTER_CACHE_TARGETS behavior and explicitly forbids "fixing" it.

---
Verification of the ten requested points

1. All BVS whole-target caches and prefix fallbacks eliminated — YES. The Decision section's four deletion targets cover all six restore sites and both save
sites I previously located across the three workflows. The hygientarget-bvs-* key, restore/save action, target digest, orrestore-keys in all three workflows. The Swatinem steps are correctly identified as registry/git-only (cache-targets: false, verified) and pinned to stay
that way.
2. S3 identities, one head-resolver digest, no bootstrap ambiguitthe current workflow: today's S3 keys share the digest step thatemits bootstrap-${GITHUB_SHA} on resolver changes; the spec removes that branch everywhere and specifies same-run key/metadata consistency. This resolves the
exact ambiguity I flagged in R1.
3. sccache authority is safe — YES, verified in code. scripts/sccache_eligibility.py grants read_write only when a write role ARN is supplied and the event is push or workflow_dispatch on refs/heads/main; PR/merge_group/schedule/dispatch otherwise resolve to read_only. Flaky-smoke's current invocation passes only the read-only role ARN — so even a manual dispatch on main stays read-only, and the spec's "flaky jobs never receive write authority" is mechanically enforceable by requiring flaky workflows not to pass write-role-arn (the hygiene matrix covers this). The action is internally fail-open: every step is continue-on-error, enabled is computed from all step outcomes, and callers gate BOLT_RUST_VERIFICATION_SCCACHE on enabled == 'true' — which is the governed
enable_env declared in both rust-verification.toml files, and manmpile_cache scrubs RUSTC_WRAPPER when disabled. Full tests are performance claim (not solved, stop and report). Whether 20% is achievable is unmeasured, and the spec correctly does not claim it.
6. Evidence conditional and executable — YES. Three-section matrix; the fail-open remote run is producible from the pre-wiring baseline head during the implementation PR's lifecycle; outage variants are unit/mutation-level.
7. Both 32 GiB files governed — YES (verified values in both files; both pinned by required tests).
8. Incident claims exact and the iteration success correctly explained — YES, verified live (see F5).
9. Runner-image risk and asymmetry honestly documented — YES (see F6/F7).
10. CI-only scope — YES. The implementation surface is workflows, hygiene verifier/tests/helpers, and telemetry script/tests; sccache authorities, input-set resolver, both policy TOMLs, storage governance, and root caches are read-only; the non-goals list excludes clock-domain, trading, S3 archival, recovery, HMAC. Sequencing keeps #1354 unbundled and puts #1367 after main becomes authoritative.

---
New findings (this revision)

N1 — LOW: One post-rollout acceptance criterion is owned by root

Evidence: The Actions-Cache Steady-State Contract requires all fow "repository listed bytes at or below 10,737,418,240," while thesame section declares root allocation/eviction separate scope. Live inventory (this session): five root entries totaling 9,118,987,793 bytes at ~1.82 GB each and churning; a sixth root entry before GitHub's LRU sweep settles would transiently list ~10.9 GB.
Consequence: This slice's acceptance can fail spuriously with zero BVS involvement, pressuring exactly the cross-scope root cleanup the spec forbids.
Required correction: Scope the acceptance assertions to the BVS fes, zero saves, no eviction/re-save loop) and make the repository
total a reported metric routed to the existing storage-tripwire oil condition of this slice.
Scope ruling: spec-text fix only; in scope.

N2 — LOW: sccache-effectiveness preconditions are unguarded after qualification

Evidence: The producer's compile steps set CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 (vn's step environment); incremental compilation defeats sccache
caching; no hygiene row covers compile-step env preconditions on
Consequence: A later innocuous env edit (dropping incremental-off, adding non-deterministic flags) silently craters hit rate; nothing fails; the qualified 20% claim rots without signal.
Required correction: Either add the known effectiveness preconditions to the hygiene contract for sccache-wired compile steps, or state explicitly that
post-qualification latency drift is accepted and observed only th monitoring.
Scope ruling: spec text plus (if adopted) the hygiene verifier/tests already in the implementation surface; in scope.

N3 — LOW: "Already governed" is demonstrated for location and authority, not for S3-side retention

Evidence: ci/sccache-location.toml owns bucket/region/prefix and sccache_eligibility.py owns roles (both verified), but I found no in-repo retention, lifecycle, or size governance for the sccache S3 location; the repo's storage tripwire covers GitHub Actions listed bytes only. Whether an AWS-side bucket lifecycle policy exists is not verifiable from the repository.
Consequence: Adding BVS compiles grows the sccache object population under a location whose byte-growth governance is not cited; "already governed" mildly overclaims.
Required correction: One sentence citing where S3-side retention/size governance for the sccache location lives, or an explicit statement that S3-side growth is pre-existing, unbounded-in-repo, marginally increased by BVS (heavy dependency overlap with root), and accepted.
Scope ruling: spec-text fix; any actual lifecycle work is separate scope and must not be pulled in.

Informational (no correction required)

- Clippy latency regression is honest but unmeasured: the clippy job loses its warm path with no replacement; the spec scopes future acceleration correctly.
Awareness only.
- Prefix misnomer: BVS compiles will store under sccache/bolt-v2/arm64/root-nextest/. Functionally safe (content-keyed, single governed location is the right invariant; renaming would orphan the existing cache), but the name becomes inaccurate. Optional one-line acknowledgment.

---
Verified facts vs. inference vs. unmeasured

Verified this session: commit and file at HEAD; all four sccache authority files exist; eligibility role logic (trusted_write on main push/dispatch with write ARN; read-only otherwise); action fail-open construction; BOLT_RUST_VERIFICATION_SCCACHE as enable_env in both policy TOMLs and its wrapper-scrub in rust_verification.py; sccache currently in ci.yml/debug-test/flaky-smoke/rust-probe and absent from backtester-ci and flaky-detection (matching "remain"/"become" phrasing); flaky-smoke passes read-only ARN only; both 32 GiB values; all three incident runs' job compositions including the iteration-run explanation; the storage numbers (re-confirmed earlier this session against the live API); the hygiene rules being inverted exist as described.

Unmeasured (spec correctly treats as such): the 20% threshold's abuild cost; S3-side sccache byte growth.

The pivot — delete the unsafe cache family outright, reuse compiles through already-governed content-keyed sccache, gate the performance claim on a measured 20% with honest stop semantics — is the right design, and this revision is notably careful about what it does not claim. FINAL N1–N3 SPEC REVIEW — Round 3

Reviewer: Claude Code CLI session, model claude-fable-5 (harness-declared ID), reasoning effort xhigh as configured for this session by the operator.
Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-12-bvs-compatibility-cache-design.md at commit 65c6b4ffe13b0cef5e23bf4511b1f153929e7368 ("docs(ci): close BVS sccache review findings", 27 insertions / 8 deletions over R2 — diff reviewed in full, changed sections read in context).
Method: read-only; every new claim in the diff verified against the repo at HEAD. Nothing modified, planned, pushed, or mutated.

Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES — one finding

N1 and N3 are resolved exactly as required and F1–F7 are untouched and intact. But the N2 correction introduced a new defect: its central requirement is mechanically inert under the repo's own managed build path, and its stated rationale is false. This is the only remaining issue.

---
N1 — RESOLVED, verified

The steady-state contract's fourth bullet is now "no BVS eviction/re-save loop across the observation window," replacing the repository-total condition. Root-family population and the repository total are explicitly "report-only metrics routed to the existing storage-tripwire owner; they are not acceptance conditions for this slice," and the threshold paragraph now states plainly that a root-churn breach "does not fail this slice when all four BVS-specific assertions remain true." The matrix's Zero-budget row and new invariant 14 match. Acceptance is now fully owned by what this slice controls.

N3 — RESOLVED, verified

The new paragraph states that the checked-in repository "does not govern or prove an S3 lifecycle rule, retention limit, or byte ceiling" for the location, that growth is "unbounded in-repo," pre-existing, and marginally increased by BVS — with the dependency-overlap expectation explicitly marked "unmeasured and is not an acceptance premise" (a precision I did not ask for and am glad to see). Lifecycle/retention work is fenced into a separate reviewed design. The root-nextest prefix is acknowledged as historical and descriptively incomplete, retained deliberately with the correct reason (renaming would split or orphan the existing cache). The former "already governed" phrasing is now "location- and authority-governed," which removes the overclaim.

N2 — the correction itself is defective

M1 — MEDIUM: The per-step CARGO_INCREMENTAL: "0" requirement is inert under the managed environment, and the paragraph's rationale is false

Evidence (all verified this session):
- SCRUB_ENV_KEYS in scripts/rust_verification.py includes CARGO_INCREMENTAL (alongside RUSTFLAGS, CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS, CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS, RUSTC_WRAPPER, RUSTC_WORKSPACE_WRAPPER).
- managed_env copies the caller environment, pops every scrub key, and never re-sets CARGO_INCREMENTAL. A workflow step that sets it therefore has zero effect on the actual cargo invocation — the value is deleted before cargo runs.
- Neither Cargo.toml nor the BVS manifest sets incremental in any profile (the only profile section is [profile.release] with lto/strip/panic).
- The three workflows already running qualified sccache compiles today — ci.yml, debug-test.yml, rust-probe.yml — set CARGO_INCREMENTAL nowhere. The value visible in the incident job's step environment came from Swatinem rust-cache's default GITHUB_ENV export — which the managed environment then scrubs anyway.
- Cargo compiles git/registry dependencies (including NautilusTrader, the dominant wall-clock cost) non-incrementally regardless of this variable; incremental applies to workspace-local crates, whose compiles sccache treats as non-cacheable either way. This is precisely why the existing root sccache path produces hits with the variable scrubbed.

Issue/consequence: The spec mandates a control that cannot influence the build, asserts that "silently dropping this value after qualification would invalidate the measured latency claim" (false — it never reaches the compiler), and contradicts itself within the same paragraph, which correctly notes that "the managed verification environment already scrubs those caller-controlled values" — the very mechanism that makes the new requirement dead. The hygiene verifier and mutation tests (matrix row "Sccache effectiveness preconditions"), invariant 13, and workflow-behavior step 4 would all enforce and advertise a knob that guards nothing. It also puts a compile-shaping decision in the workflow layer when the repo's design deliberately makes the managed environment own that surface.

Required correction (either branch is acceptable; pick one and make the text, invariant 13, step 4, and the matrix row consistent with it):
1. Own it in the owning layer: have the managed environment guarantee non-incremental compiles — managed_env (or the remote-compile-cache env composition) sets CARGO_INCREMENTAL="0", unconditionally for managed runs or gated on remote-compile-cache/CI enablement, with unit tests. scripts/rust_verification.py and its test file are already in the implementation surface, so this fits declared scope (if a policy-TOML key is preferred over a code constant, the TOMLs must move out of the read-only authority list — say so explicitly). Hygiene then checks nothing at the workflow layer for incremental.
2. Document reality and drop the requirement: state that CARGO_INCREMENTAL is scrubbed by the managed environment, that dependency compiles (the dominant, cacheable cost) are non-incremental by cargo behavior, and that workspace-local compiles are uncached — identical to the already-operating root sccache path the qualification is modeled on.

What in the N2 section survives as-is: the profile-debug preservation requirements are effective and verified (CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG/CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG are not in the scrub list and are present exactly where the spec says — producer archive/sidecar steps and all flaky-smoke BVS steps, with "where they already exist" correctly describing flaky-detection's lack of them); the raw-flag/wrapper ban is accurate defense-in-depth, since all five named variables are genuinely scrubbed and the wrapper is genuinely owned by managed_remote_compile_cache_env; and the requalify-on-compile-input-change rule is sound. The sentence "the current Backtester producer does not set it" is literally true of the workflow file. Only the incremental requirement, its rationale sentence, and their echoes in invariant 13, step 4, and the matrix row need rework.

F1–F7 preservation: CONFIRMED

The diff touches none of the sections carrying the prior resolutions — incident evidence with exact runs/heads and the iteration-run explanation, the S3 identity and hygiene-inversion contract, the zero-target-cache decision, both 32 GiB authorities, the conditional three-section matrix structure, the runner-image residual, and the asymmetry section are all byte-identical or strengthened. Invariants 1–12 are unchanged; 13–14 are additions. The implementation-surface edits only extend the hygiene verifier/tests lines to cover the new preconditions, consistent with scope.

---
Bottom line: REQUEST CHANGES on M1 only. This is a one-section rewrite with two clearly specified acceptable resolutions — everything else in the spec is verified, coherent, honestly claimed, and preserves all ten prior resolutions. On the evidence already gathered, resolving M1 is the last step before this spec is approvable.

Design Spec Review — BVS Compile-Compatibility Cache

Reviewer: Claude Code CLI session, model claude-fable-5 (harness-declared ID), reasoning effort xhigh as configured for this session by the operator (the effort setting is operator-configured; the model ID is the verifiable anchor).
Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-12-bvs-compatibility-cache-design.md at commit 98764840a3fb073320682a24502ef97d776a2b81 (worktree HEAD verified to match).
Method: read-only. Every claim below is anchored to a file read at the pinned commit, a live GitHub API read, or the actual failed CI run log. No files edited, no Rust compiled, nothing mutated.

Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES

The core design — exact compatibility-seed keys, no prefix fallback, main-only writes, truthful telemetry, budget-gated with disabled-cache as the honest default — is sound, and its factual claims are unusually accurate (I verified the cache inventory byte-for-byte and replayed the incident from the real run log). But three findings are substantive spec gaps that would force the implementer to make unreviewed contract decisions or produce impossible evidence. They are fixable in the spec witho
F1 — MAJOR: The fate of the shared digest step (and the bootstrap namespace for S3 keys) is unspecified

Evidence (verified): In backtester-ci.yml, one step (bvs_cache_inputs, the "Compute BVS cache input hash" step) produces a single digest consumed by both the managed-target cache keys and the S3 nextest/sidecar keys (bvs-nextest-archive-v4-..., bvs-bin-sidecars-v4-...). When the resolver or its TOML changes, that step emits bootstrap-${GITHUB_SHA} — so today, S3 keys also get a commit-SHA identity in that case. On top of that, the current hygiene verifier mandates both things the design bans: backtester_managed_target_cache_errors in scripts/verify_ci_workflow_hygiene.py requires every BVS cache key to use that one shared digest step, and requires the bootstrap-SHA branch to exist in backtester-ci.yml ("backtester cache key digest must use exact-head namespace when CI input-set bootstrap changes").

Issue: The spec says the verifier "must fail if it finds bootstrap-${GITHUB_SHA}, any other commit-SHA namespace" — with ambiguous scope — and says S3 keys "continue to use the full-source backtester_cache digest," which misdescribes today's behavior (they don't, in the bootstrap case). Splitting the digest step is mechanically forced (target keys need the new seed; S3 keys need the full-source digest), but the spec never states what the S3 keys do when the resolver changes once the bootstrap branch is gone. An implementer keeping bootstrap for S3 keys and a hygiene rule banning all commit-SHA namespaces are mutually contradictory.

Required correction: Specify explicitly: (a) two separate digest steps with per-family key rules in the hygiene contract; (b) the S3 key/metadata identity in the resolver-change case (the safe answer: always the head-resolver backtester_cache digest, bootstrap namespace eliminated everywhere, with a sentence on why save/restore-at-same-head keeps the metadata contract intact); (c) an explicit note that two existing verifier rules are being inverted/split, so the mutation tests target the right invariants.

F2 — MAJOR: The budget gate measures a snapshot; steady-state coexistence with the root cache family is unmodeled

Evidence (measured live during this review): The repository's five current cache entries are all root-lane managed-target-v1-Linux-ARM64-test-archive-test-<digest> caches, ~1.82 GiB each, five distinct digests accessed within roughly 36 hours — the root family alone churns near the entire 10 GiB budget. The 6,419,854,292-byte BVS v3 cache that was restored at 08:11 UTC today is already gone from the inventory (LRU-evicted).

Issue: The rollout gate (spec steps 1–4) accepts on pre-save headroom plus one post-save snapshot. It does not account for: (a) root-family churn continuously refilling the budget; (b) GitHub's LRU eviction, which would repeatedly evict an accepted ~6.4 GiB BVS cache and cause it to be re-saved on each subsequent main push — permanent upload/storage thrash that a snapshot-passing rollout would misreport as success; (c) seed transitions (every NT-pin/lockfile change) leaving the prior seed's entries alive until eviction/expiry, so the family transiently holds two seeds per lane, which the criterion-2 projection doesn't count.

Required correction: Make the gate a steady-state projection — BVS family (both lanes) + 2× seed-transition transient + the measured root-family churn window must fit under 10 GiB — and add a post-rollout stability criterion (e.g., no eviction-driven BVS re-save across N main pushes, verified from the existing storage-audit evidence). Note this
Evidence (verified in spec text): The "Rust and workflow behavior" row requires "a later same-seed source-only head gets an exact hit" as mandatory remote evidence, while the budget section states "the default outcome is the disabled-cache fallback." Under the fallback there are no BVS target-cache steps, so an exact hit can never be observed. The matrix has a "Fallback" row but never scopes the enabled-lane rows as conditional.

Issue: Read literally, the implementation cannot satisfy its own mandatory evidence table in the default path — and under AGENTS.md, every requirement must map to producible evidence.

Required correction: Split the matrix into "enabled-lane evidence" and "fallback evidence" sets, each binding only for the branch actually selected by the budget gate, with the gate's own measurement as the recorded selector.

F4 — LOW: The 32 GiB authority citation names only half of the governing policy

Evidence (verified): The BVS lanes run rust_verification.py run --repo crates/backtesting-vertical-slice (the bte-clippy/bte-test recipes), so the operative policy file for the incident lane is crates/backtesting-vertical-slice/ci/rust-verification.toml (soft_limit_bytes = 32212254720 at its [cache] section). The spec's telemetry section and read-only authority list cite only root ci/rust-verification.toml (same value). Relatedly, the spec's description of backtester_cache as including "build policy" holds only for the BVS-local policy file — the root one is not a member of that set.

Required correction: Cite both files as 32 GiB authorities and require the "32 GiB limit retained" test to pin both values, so drift in the file that actually governs the lane can't slip past a root-only assertion.

F5 — LOW: "Twice on the same head" is not anchored, and an interleaved success is unexplained

Evidence (verified): At PR #1367's current head ff174ca18 there is exactly one Backtester CI run (29185376240, single attempt). I verified that run's failure mode end-to-end from its log: exact miss → prefix restore of managed-target-bvs-v3-Linux-ARM64-test-bootstrap-9f3b13f4c... (6,419,854,292 bytes — a per-commit bootstrap cache saved from main, which is a nice confirmation that the bootstrap namespace itself was the poison source) → build → disk_pressure refusal at the binary-sidecar preflight with free_bytes: 79150555136 and reclaimable_bytes: 0, exactly as the spec describes. The earlier head a9b4fc62e has two Backtester failures and one success between them (runs 29177797488 failure, 29180025916 success, 29180923906 failure); I did not verify those two failures' mode individually.

Issue: The incident section overstates reproducibility precision — a reviewer replaying "twice on the same head" at the PR's current head finds one occurrence, and the same-head success undermines the narrative if left unaddressed.

Required correction: Pin the incident claims to exact run IDs and head SHAs, and either explain the interleaved success (duplicate-event run with skipped jobs, or S3 archive hit bypassing the build) or soften to "repeatedly across PR #1367 heads."

F6 — LOW: Runner-image residual ri
Issue: A runner-image change (glibc, system libraries) keeps both the seed and the key constant while potentially invalidating compiled-artifact compatibility. The seed's claim to include "every checked-in surface" is literally true — but this not-checked-in surface survives from v3 into v4 unmentioned, and the spec's framing invites the reader to believe compatibility is fully keyed.

Required correction: One paragraph acknowledging the residual, and the accepted rationale (worst case is a failed build caught by CI, not a silently wrong artifact — or add an image identifier to the key if one is reliably available).

F7 — INFO: State the resolver-policy asymmetry so it isn't "fixed" by the implementer

FORBIDDEN_BACKTESTER_CACHE_TARGETS in scripts/ci_input_sets.py hard-forbids backtester_cache from covering the workflow file, the resolver, its TOML, and the setup action — three of which the new seed requires as members. Set-scoped, so not a contradiction, but the spec's self-test section should say the seed's membership policy is intentionally inverse on those paths.

---
Verified facts vs. inference vs. unmeasured

Verified facts (all anchors personally re-read/re-run at HEAD or fetched live this session): all 30 files the spec names exist at the pinned commit; the v3 keys and broad restore-keys prefixes are exactly at the cited sites in all three workflows; saves are already main-only and flaky lanes already restore-only (the writer-authority section retains current behavior — accurate, though the spec presents it as new design); digest_input_set does hash ordered pathspec declarations plus tracked bytes, so the deterministic-bootstrap claim holds; both 32 GiB values and the 10 GiB cache-listed-bytes warning threshold are exact; the live inventory matches the spec byte-for-byte (5 entries, 9,118,987,793 total, 1,618,430,447 headroom, no surviving BVS entry); the 6,419,854,292-byte prior cache is confirmed from the run log; today's refusal payload contains no measured-target-size field and hardcodes reclaimable_bytes: 0, so the telemetry redesign fixes a real, confirmed gap; the hash <set_name> CLI shape is correct; the slice is CI-only, bundles no clock-domain code, and unblocks #1367 in both outcomes (a fallback cold build starts from an empty target, so the 32 GiB refusal can't re-trigger from restored payload). No conflict with AGENTS.md found.

Inference (reasonable, not directly measured): the 32 GiB overage being caused by the mixture of two dependency graphs — well-supported by the 6.4 GiB compressed restore plus a full new NT graph compile, and the spec itself correctly refuses to claim byte apportionment.

Unmeasured (spec should keep treating as such): the clippy-lane cache size (spec's 6.42 GiB proxy is reasonable but per-lane sizes are unknown until first save); whether a clean cold-build target under the new NT pin stays below 32 GiB (correctly covered by the matrix's "cold miss succeeds" requirement).

---
Bottom line: REQUEST CHANGES on F1–F3 (F4–F7 should be folded into the same revision). The design's decisions are right and its evidence discipline is genuinely good — the incident narrative survived a full adversarial replay against the real run log, which is rare. Resolve the three gaps in the spec text and it is fit to gate a plan. Per you relay the findings so the spec is corrected and resubmitted for re-review before any planning. (disable recaps in /config)

Verification of the ten requested points

1. All BVS whole-target caches and prefix fallbacks eliminated — YES. The Decision section's four deletion targets cover all six restore sites and both save sites I previously located across the three workflows. The hygiene matrix bans any managed-target-bvs-* key, restore/save action, target digest, or restore-keys in all three workflows. The Swatinem steps are correctly identified as registry/git-only (cache-targets: false, verified) and pinned to stay that way.
2. S3 identities, one head-resolver digest, no bootstrap ambiguity — YES. Verified against the current workflow: today's S3 keys share the digest step that emits bootstrap-${GITHUB_SHA} on resolver changes; the spec removes that branch everywhere and specifies same-run key/metadata consistency. This resolves the exact ambiguity I flagged in R1.
3. sccache authority is safe — YES, verified in code. scripts/sccache_eligibility.py grants read_write only when a write role ARN is supplied and the event is push or workflow_dispatch on refs/heads/main; PR/merge_group/schedule/dispatch otherwise resolve to read_only. Flaky-smoke's current invocation passes only the read-only role ARN — so even a manual dispatch on main stays read-only, and the spec's "flaky jobs never receive write authority" is mechanically enforceable by requiring flaky workflows not to pass write-role-arn (the hygiene matrix covers this). The action is internally fail-open: every step is continue-on-error, enabled is computed from all step outcomes, and callers gate BOLT_RUST_VERIFICATION_SCCACHE on enabled == 'true' — which is the governed enable_env declared in both rust-verification.toml files, and managed_env_without_remote_compile_cache scrubs RUSTC_WRAPPER when disabled. Full tests are preserved (workflow step 7, invariformula over GitHub-reported compile-step durations, hit/error counters from the existing sccache-stats action (verified present and already used by four workflows), explicit stop semantics on a miss with no limit-raising and no reinterpretation of hit counts as wall-clock. Note the gate is necessarily post-merge (main-only writes); a sub-20% result cannot retroactively remove the safety fix — the spec handles this honestly by separating the correctness claim (solved by removal) from the performance claim (not solved, stop and report). Whether 20% is achievable is unmeasured, and the spec correctly does not claim it.
6. Evidence conditional and executable — YES. Three-section matrix; the fail-open remote run is producible from the pre-wiring baseline head during the implementation PR's lifecycle; outage variants are unit/mutation-level.
7. Both 32 GiB files governed — YES (verified values in both files; both pinned by required tests).
8. Incident claims exact and the iteration success correctly explained — YES, verified live (see F5).
9. Runner-image risk and asymmetry honestly documented — YES (see F6/F7).
10. CI-only scope — YES. The implementation surface is workflows, hygiene verifier/tests/helpers, and telemetry script/tests; sccache authorities, input-set resolver, both policy TOMLs, storage governance, and root caches are read-only; the non-goals list excludes clock-domain, trading, S3 archival, recovery, HMAC. Sequencing keeps #1354 unbundled and puts #1367 after main becomes authoritative.

New findings (this revision)

N1 — LOW: One post-rollout acceptance criterion is owned by root churn, not this slice

Evidence: The Actions-Cache Steady-State Contract requires all four inventory points to show "repository listed bytes at or below 10,737,418,240," while the same section declares root allocation/eviction separate scope. Live inventory (this session): five root entries totaling 9,118,987,793 bytes at ~1.82 GB each and churning; a sixth root entry before GitHub's LRU sweep settles would transiently list ~10.9 GB.
Consequence: This slice's acceptance can fail spuriously with zero BVS involvement, pressuring exactly the cross-scope root cleanup the spec forbids.
Required correction: Scope the acceptance assertions to the BVS family (zero keys, zero bytes, zero saves, no eviction/re-save loop) and make the repository total a reported metric routed to the existing storage-tripwire owner rather than a pass/fail condition of this slice.
Scope ruling: spec-text fix only; in scope.

N2 — LOW: sccache-effectiveness preconditions are unguarded after qualification

Evidence: The producer's compile steps set CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 (verified in the incident run's step environment); incremental compilation defeats sccache caching; no hygiene row covers compile-step env preconditions on the sccache-wired steps.
Consequence: A later innocuous env edit (dropping incremental-off, adding non-deterministic flags) silently craters hit rate; nothing fails; the qualified 20% claim rots without signal.
Required correction: Either add the known effectiveness preconditions to the hygiene contract for sccache-wired compile steps, or state explicitly that post-qualification latency drift is accepted and observed only through ordinary CI-duration monitoring.
Scope ruling: spec text plus (if alifecycle, or size governance for the sccache S3 location; the repo's storage tripwire covers GitHub Actions listed bytes only. Whether an AWS-side bucket lifecycle policy exists is not verifiable from the repository.
Consequence: Adding BVS compiles grows the sccache object population under a location whose byte-growth governance is not cited; "already governed" mildly overclaims.
Required correction: One sentence citing where S3-side retention/size governance for the sccache location lives, or an explicit statement that S3-side growth is pre-existing, unbounded-in-repo, marginally increased by BVS (heavy dependency overlap with root), and accepted.
Scope ruling: spec-text fix; any actual lifecycle work is separate scope and must not be pulled in.

Informational (no correction required)

- Clippy latency regression is honest but unmeasured: the clippy job loses its warm path with no replacement; the spec scopes future acceleration correctly. Awareness only.
- Prefix misnomer: BVS compiles will store under sccache/bolt-v2/arm64/root-nextest/. Functionally safe (content-keyed, single governed location is the right invariant; renaming would orphan the existing cache), but the name becomes inaccurate. Optional one-line acknowledgment.

Verified this session: commit and file at HEAD; all four sccache authority files exist; eligibility role logic (trusted_write on main push/dispatch with write ARN; read-only otherwise); action fail-open construction; BOLT_RUST_VERIFICATION_SCCACHE as enable_env in both policy TOMLs and its wrapper-scrub in rust_verification.py; sccache currently in ci.yml/debug-test/flaky-smoke/rust-probe and absent from backtester-ci and flaky-detection (matching "remain"/"become" phrasing); flaky-smoke passes read-only ARN only; both 32 GiB values; all three incident runs' job compositions including the iteration-run explanation; the storage numbers (re-confirmed earlier this session against the live API); the hygiene rules being inverted exist as described.

Method: read-only; every new claim in the diff verified against the repo at HEAD. Nothing modified, planned, pushed, or mutated.

Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES — one finding

N1 — RESOLVED, verified

The steady-state contract's fourth bullet is now "no BVS eviction/re-save loop across the observation window," replacing the repository-total condition. Root-family population and the repository total are explicitly "report-only metrics routed to the existing storage-tripwire owner; they are not acceptance conditions for this slice," and the threshold paragraph now states plainly that a root-churn breach "does not fail this slice when all four BVS-specific assertions remain true." The matrix's Zero-budget row and new invariant 14 match. Acceptance is now fully owned by what this slice controls.

N3 — RESOLVED, verified

The new paragraph states that the checked-in repository "does not govern or prove an S3 lifecycle rule, retention limit, or byte ceiling" for the location, that growth is "unbounded in-repo," pre-existing, and marginally increased by BVS — with the dependency-overlap expectation explicitly marked "unmeasured and is not an acceptance premise" (a precision I did not ask for and am glad to see). Lifecycle/retention work is fenced into a separate reviewed design. The root-nextest prefix is acknowledged as historical and descriptively incomplete, retained deliberately with the correct reason (renaming would split or orphan the existing cache). The former "already governed" phrasing is now "location- and authority-governed," which removes the overclaim.

N2 — the correction itself is defective

M1 — MEDIUM: The per-step CARGO_INCREMENTAL: "0" requirement is inert under the managed environment, and the paragraph's rationale is false

Evidence (all verified this session):
- SCRUB_ENV_KEYS in scripts/rust_verification.py includes CARGO_INCREMENTAL (alongside RUSTFLAGS, CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS, CARGO_BUILD_RUSTFLAGS, RUSTC_WRAPPER, RUSTC_WORKSPACE_WRAPPER).
- managed_env copies the caller environment, pops every scrub key, and never re-sets CARGO_INCREMENTAL. A workflow step that sets it therefore has zero effect on the actual cargo invocation — the value is deleted before cargo runs.
- Neither Cargo.toml nor the BVS manifest sets incremental in any profile (the only profile section is [profile.release] with lto/strip/panic).
- The three workflows already running qualified sccache compiles today — ci.yml, debug-test.yml, rust-probe.yml — set CARGO_INCREMENTAL nowhere. The value visible in the incident job's step environment came from Swatinem rust-cache's default GITHUB_ENV export — which the managed environment then scrubs anyway.
- Cargo compiles git/registry dependencies (including NautilusTrader, the dominant wall-clock cost) non-incrementally regardless of this variable; incremental applies to workspace-local crates, whose compiles sccache treats as non-cacheable either way. This is precisely why the existing root sccache path produces hits with the variable scrubbed.

Issue/consequence: The spec mandates a control that cannot influence the build, asserts that "silently dropping this value after qualification would invalidate the measured latency claim" (false — it never reaches the compiler), and contradicts itself within the same paragraph, which correctly notes that "the managed verification environment already scrubs those caller-controlled values" — the very mechanism that makes the new requirement dead. The hygiene verifier and mutation tests (matrix row "Sccache effectiveness preconditions"), invariant 13, and workflow-behavior step 4 would all enforce and advertise a knob that guards nothing. It also sets CARGO_INCREMENTAL="0", unconditionally for managed runs or gated on remote-compile-cache/CI enablement, with unit tests. scripts/rust_verification.py and its test file are already in the implementation surface, so this fits declared scope (if a policy-TOML key is preferred over a code constant, the TOMLs must move out of the read-only authority list — say so explicitly). Hygiene then checks nothing at the workflow layer for incremental.
2. Document reality and drop the requirement: state that CARGO_INCREMENTAL is scrubbed by the managed environment, that dependency compiles (the dominant, cacheable cost) are non-incremental by cargo behavior, and that workspace-local compiles are uncached — identical to the already-operating root sccache path the qualification is modeled on.

What in the N2 section survives as-is: the profile-debug preservation requirements are effective and verified (CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG/CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG are not in the scrub list and are present exactly where the spec says — producer archive/sidecar steps and all flaky-smoke BVS steps, with "where they already exist" correctly describing flaky-detection's lack of them); the raw-flag/wrapper ban is accurate defense-in-depth, since all five named variables are genuinely scrubbed and the wrapper is genuinely owned by managed_remote_compile_cache_env; and the requalify-on-compile-input-change rule is sound. The sentence "the current Backtester producer does not set it" is literally true of the workflow file. Only the incremental requirement, its rationale sentence, and their echoes in invariant 13, step 4, and the matrix row need rework.

F1–F7 preservation: CONFIRMED

The diff touches none of the sections carrying the prior resolutions — incident evidence with exact runs/heads and the iteration-run explanation, the S3 identity and hygiene-inversion contract, the zero-target-cache decision, both 32 GiB authorities, the conditional three-section matrix structure, the runner-image residual, and the asymmetry section are all byte-identical or strengthened. Invariants 1–12 are unchanged; 13–14 are additions. The implementation-surface edits only extend the hygiene verifier/tests lines to cover the new preconditions, consistent with scope.

This is a one-section rewrite with two clearly specified acceptable resolutions — everything else in the spec is verified, coherent, honestly claimed, and preserves all ten prior resolutions. On the evidence already gathered, resolving M1 is the last step before this spec is approvable.

---
Findings

1 — MEDIUM · the behavioral proof is not bound to NautilusTrader

Evidence: scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py — the string nautilus_binance appears 0 times in the entire 513-line fence. BINANCE_TIMESTAMP_TEST_CASE_REQUIREMENTS (lines 79+) validates the harness body with text regexes only, e.g. ("parse_bbo_event call", r"\bparse_bbo_event\s*\("). It requires that a function named parse_bbo_event is called — never that it is NT's. The verifier's own clean fixture binance_timestamp_test_source() (test_verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py:59) contains zero use statements and passes with no findings, which is the fence certifying by construction that NT linkage is not required.

Consequence: delete the use nautilus_binance::{…} block from tests/binance_sbe_quote_timestamps.rs and define local stub parse_* functions returning a local struct. Every fence stays green, --no-tests=fail stays green (the tests still exist and still pass), full CI stays green — and the NT SBE parsers are never executed. The proof becomes a tautology while governance reports success. This is precisely the failure mode review question 3 asks to rule out, and the answer is no.

Required correction (class, not patch): in scan_binance_timestamp_behavioral_contract, require the exact import path nautilus_binance::spot::websocket::streams::parse::{parse_bbo_event, parse_depth_diff, parse_depth_snapshot, parse_trades_event} (plus parse_spot_instrument_sbe and nautilus_model::data::Data), and reject any local fn/struct definition in the harness whose name collides with a governed symbol. Add the negative test.

Scope: in scope — this PR introduces the fence and makes the claim.

2 — MEDIUM · the pin census enumerates instead of discovering

Evidence: scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py:48-57 — PIN_SURFACES is a hardcoded 8-tuple.

Consequence: the census governs the files it was told about. A new crate with its own Cargo.toml/Cargo.lock pinning NT is entirely ungoverned, and could pin the old SHA with every fence green. This is not hypothetical: root Cargo.toml has no [workspace] section and crates/backtesting-vertical-slice is already a separate workspace with its own lockfile — the second-workspace pattern is established here, so the third one will be ungoverned by default. A verifier whose completeness depends on someone remembering to update a tuple is a fence that silently narrows over time.

Required correction (class): derive the census by globbing **/Cargo.toml + **/Cargo.lock (excluding target/) and fail closed if any file referencing nautilus_trader.git or a nautilus-* package is not in PIN_SURFACES.

Scope: in scope.

3 — MEDIUM · undisclosed live behavior change: BTC RV same-millisecond dedupe flips from first-wins to last-wins

Evidence (verified at head, in code this PR does not touch): RealizedVolatilityRuntime::observe_quote (src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility_runtime.rs) maps quote.ts_event → event_ts_ms and quote.ts_init → recv_ts_ms. reject_observation (src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs) then compares, for two observations sharing a venue event millisecond: observed_receive_ts == prior_receive_ts → DuplicateTimestamp (reject); < → StaleSameEventUpdate (reject); otherwise accept — and RealizedVolEngine::observe does pop_back() then push_back(), replacing the prior same-event sample.

Before this pin, Binance Spot SBE emitted ts_init == ts_event, so two BBO updates in the same venue millisecond had identical recv_ts_ms → always DuplicateTimestamp → the first quote in that millisecond won. After the pin, recv_ts_ms are distinct monotonic local receipts → accepted → the last quote in that millisecond wins. grid_prices samples the last sample with event_ts_ms <= t, so this changes the actual midpoints the RV estimator consumes at grid boundaries.

Consequence: a real, unannounced change to the live BTC RV input series → RV → RV gate → admission. I judge the new behavior correct (it is what the DuplicateTimestamp/StaleSameEventUpdate design presumes, and it brings Binance into line with OKX and Bybit, which already stamped local ts_init at afc014a55). But the PR body presents this as a timestamp-ownership and pin slice with no runtime consequence, and it lands ahead of #1354.

Required correction: state this behavior change explicitly in the PR body — it is the one place the corrected ts_init is a live decision input, not evidence. No code change needed.

Scope: consequence of the pin, not a bundled change. Disclosure, not code.

4 — LOW · asymmetric negative test on the new registry rows

REQUIRED_BINANCE_WS_REGISTRY_ENTRIES mandates both RealizedVolatilityObservation and StrategySignalObservation, but the only removal test (test_missing_binance_live_quote_feeder_fails_closed) deletes the StrategySignalObservation row. Same code path, so coverage is real, but add the symmetric case. (BoundaryEvidenceClass::WebSocketFrame is correct for an SBE binary frame — the enum classifies transport, not encoding.)

---
Notable non-finding (for the record)

On the signal-trigger path, every gate that can admit or block a trade reads ts_event, which this PR does not change: the jitter-eligibility gate and spike cooldown in bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs (record_signal_quote_timing, detect_signal_spike), and the exit-evaluation clock (after_signal_quote_observed passes venue observed_ts_ms as now_ms). The corrected ts_init reaches only trigger_ts_init_ms in decision evidence. So the answer to review question 2(a) is: the signal path consumes the corrected stamp, but only as evidence — the behavior change is confined to the RV path (finding 3). No live code computes ts_init - ts_event as a latency gate, sorts by ts_init, or otherwise relies on their equality.

  - Issue: A module-level macro_rules! assert_eq, assert_ne, and assert can shadow Rust’s standard
    assertion macros while leaving every governed parser call, direct result binding, and required
    assertion-shaped token sequence intact. The custom macros can ignore their arguments.

    redirectable/no-op-able despite the new result-binding protection.

    as ::core::assert_eq!, ::core::assert_ne!, and ::core::assert!, require those exact paths in the
    verifier, and add a negative mutation covering local macro shadowing. An equally strong explicit
    prohibition on assertion macro definitions/imports would also work.

RULING: REQUEST CHANGES

Two blocking findings. The upstream change, the pin census, the RV consequence, the #1371 integration, and all six exact-head runs verify clean — but the parser-result fence, which is the load-bearing guarantee of the whole behavioral proof, does not enforce the contract it claims, and I demonstrated it with working bypasses on all four cases.

---
BLOCKING

B1 — Value-substituting postfix laundering bypasses the parser-result fence (all 4 governed cases)

Evidence (verified, empirical). has_governed_binance_parser_result_contract in scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py matches the parser call with a regex that ends at adapter_ts_init\s*\) and has no terminator anchor. Anything appended after the call is invisible to it. I built a scratchpad harness driving scan_binance_timestamp_behavioral_contract at the exact PR head against mutated copies of the real harness. Confirmed bypasses producing zero findings:

- trades: let trades = nt_binance_sbe_parse::parse_trades_event(&event, &instrument, adapter_ts_init).fabricate(expected_ts_event, adapter_ts_init);
- BBO: same shape on parse_bbo_event
- depth snapshot / depth diff: …parse_depth_snapshot(&event, &instrument, adapter_ts_init).map(|_| fabricated_deltas(expected_ts_event, adapter_ts_init)).expect("…")

A postfix method that discards the parsed value and returns a hand-built one carrying exactly the stamps the assertions expect passes the fence, passes the assertions, and passes CI.

Consequence. The verifier's own finding text promises the result "must bind … directly to pinned <parser> … without rebinding or reassignment." It does not enforce that. The fence rejects prefix wrapping (identity(...), Some(...)), destructuring, rebinding, aliasing, and macro shadowing — but a postfix chain is the one laundering direction left open, and it is the easiest one to write. Because the mutation suite has no postfix coverage (scripts/test_verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py greps clean for postfix/.map(/.clone()), the gap is untested as well as unenforced. This is a governance defect, not a live-code defect: the committed harness at head is canonical (my control run is clean), so the behavioral proof at this head is genuine.

Required correction. Anchor the parser-result binding to the statement's full initializer: require the RHS token sequence to be exactly the pinned parser call, permitting only an allowlisted .expect("…") for the two Option-returning parsers (which is why prefix-matching exists — parse_depth_snapshot/parse_depth_diff legitimately use it). Reject every other postfix chain. Add mutation tests covering .clone(), .map(|_| …), and a trait-method postfix on all four cases.

Scope ruling. In scope. This is the PR's own tamper-evidence surface (scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py + its test file are 3,086 of the PR's added lines), not adjacent work.

B2 — PR body evidence is stale relative to head, and the internal approval does not cover the defect-carrying commits

Evidence (verified). The body states current exact head: cecbeee263724abed0a5f16c09ebb3fcad40fad1, that "fresh root and BVS exact-head CI for the current head is pending," and that "independent internal adversarial review approved exact local head cecbeee26… with no findings." The actual head is 1bbd64a4d, three commits later: f99be6715 ("keep timestamp assertions reachable"), 1e67eed46 ("close Binance timestamp proof gaps"), 1bbd64a4d ("bind timestamp assertions to statements").

Consequence. Two distinct problems. First, the CI claim under-reports reality — exact-head CI is in fact green (I verified it below), so the body understates rather than overclaims. Second, and materially: those three unreviewed commits are precisely the ones that rewrote the assertion-reachability and statement-binding logic — the same code that carries B1. The internal adversarial approval therefore does not cover the code containing the defect, which is exactly how B1 survived to head.

Required correction. Re-anchor the body's head, verification, and internal-review claims to 1bbd64a4d (or to the head after B1 is fixed), and have the internal adversarial review cover the commits it currently omits.

Scope ruling. In scope — evidence integrity is explicitly part of this review's purpose.

GPT: SOURCE RULING: REQUEST CHANGES

  ## Finding

  ### High — parser proof does not bind ::nautilus_binance to the pinned Cargo package

  - Evidence: The verifier requires the textual import at verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L89-L92

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/c8e7af4be0b6d4de6860959834f8132ed12894b9/scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L89-L92),
    but its identity check only governs the nt_binance_sbe_parse alias and parser-symbol occurrences at
    #L1280-L1315

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/c8e7af4be0b6d4de6860959834f8132ed12894b9/scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L1280-L1315).
    Meanwhile, the pin census explicitly accepts renamed dependencies based on package or Git identity at
    #L914-L928

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/c8e7af4be0b6d4de6860959834f8132ed12894b9/scripts/verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L914-L928).

  - Issue: In Rust 2018+, leading :: resolves through the extern prelude, whose names come from dependency
    aliases—not directly from package identity. A mutation can:
      1. retain the reviewed nautilus-binance package under another dependency alias;
      2. provide an unrelated path package under the dependency key nautilus_binance;
      3. keep the exact governed use ::nautilus_binance::... as nt_binance_sbe_parse;;
      4. place fabricated parser functions outside the governed harness.

    The pin census still sees the retained pinned package, while the harness verifier sees the required
    spelling and qualified calls. The archive would execute the fabricated crate. The current mutation
    test at test_verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L1572-L1598

    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/c8e7af4be0b6d4de6860959834f8132ed12894b9/scripts/test_verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py#L1572-L1598)
    only tests local shadowing of the parser alias/symbols, not Cargo-level crate-name substitution.
    Rust’s extern-prelude resolution is documented in the Rust Reference
    (https://doc.rust-lang.org/stable/reference/paths.html#path-qualifiers).

  - Consequence: Governance can remain green while all four timestamp assertions operate on fabricated
    output. The behavioral proof therefore does not yet guarantee execution of the reviewed NT parser
    package.

  - Required correction: Bind the proof import name to package identity. At minimum:
      - require canonical root dependency key nautilus-binance to identify the exact pinned package and
        Git revision;

      - reject another dependency whose crate name normalizes to nautilus_binance;
      - reject extern crate self as nautilus_binance or equivalent crate-identity substitution;
      - add a cross-surface negative mutation with a retained aliased real pin plus a fake
        nautilus_binance dependency/module, proving the verifier fails.

  - Scope ruling: In scope and blocking. This directly concerns the claimed parser-proof integrity, not
    production timestamp behavior.

  ## Other reviewed areas

  Verified clean:

  - Current harness result initializers are exact pinned-parser calls; snapshot/diff add exactly one
    canonical .expect(...). Assertions directly consume trades, quote, and deltas, with aggregate and
    inner depth coverage.

  - Assertions are absolute ::core::assert*! complete statements. Branch, closure, wrapper, early-exit,
    conditional-compilation, rebinding, destructuring, raw-identifier, postfix, macro, comment/string-
    decoy, and malformed-pattern defenses are otherwise substantive.

  - Token-level whitespace/comments are tolerated around governed syntax; the depth .expect message itself
    is deliberately exact.

  - The exact-head verifier test file contains 81 tests. Exact-head source-fence-static run 29237429288
    (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/actions/runs/29237429288) logged
    test_verify_bolt_v3_boundary_evidence.py successful.

   2. Exit deduplication can oscillate across trigger sources

   • Severity: Medium
   • Evidence: src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/mod.rs:6952-6975 defines ExitOutcomeKey as {
     exit_decision, submission_blocked_reason, rv_gate_result }. rv_gate_result is computed by
     classify_rv_gate in src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:505-530 from the trigger’s
     realized_vol_gate_receive_ms against the surface’s latest_accepted_receive_ms. Different trigger
     sources carry different receive stamps (e.g. SelectionUpdate uses now_ms, SignalQuote uses ts_init,
     BookDelta uses its own ts_init; mod.rs:7784, 7865, 978). The key deliberately excludes trigger
     source and venue timestamps, but it still includes the gate result derived from those timestamps.
   • Issue: Cross-stream receive skew can make the same position/market state classify as
     RejectedFutureDated (Hold) on one trigger and Accepted (possibly Exit) on another, so the flood-gate
     key flips and both evaluations are recorded.
   • Consequence: Exit evidence / flood-gate records are not monotonic across trigger sources or clock
     skew, contrary to the review objective. Actual duplicate orders are prevented by the exposure state
     machine (exposure.exit_pending() at mod.rs:5523-5526), so the trading path remains fail-closed.
   • Required correction: Either make ExitOutcomeKey depend on a clock-independent quantity (e.g. a
     monotonic per-surface high-water receive mark), or document that re-record-on-flip is acceptable
     because downstream order submission is gated by exposure state.
   • Scope ruling: In scope — the PR claims to make exit deduplication stable across trigger sources.

        (https://github.com/seungpyoson/bolt-v2/blob/ef48cb750af0c24ed5cde2016b656c10514bf9e8/src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/mod.rs#L3598-L3608),

      - Issue: Book and selection triggers use the local strategy clock, while signal triggers use the
        independently clocked venue event timestamp for lifecycle evaluation.

      - Consequence: Venue clock lead/lag or alternating trigger sources can cross expiry/staleness
        boundaries, change exit/block outcomes, and therefore oscillate the durable dedupe key.

        observed_ts_ms only as event evidence. Add production-route tests varying venue event time while
        holding local evaluation time fixed.

Findings
No substantive findings (source)
The clock-domain reconstruction is correct, coherent, and comprehensively tested. No blocking or non-blocking source defect was identified. Two scope-aligned observations are recorded below as non-issues, not findings.
Objective 1 — Clock-domain correctness: SOUND
- classify_rv_gate (bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:1028) now compares snapshot.latest_accepted_receive_ms (receive domain) against Option<LocalReceiveMs> evaluation stamp. Venue as_of_ms is no longer in the freshness decision — it remains RV-math coordinate + evidence only.
- Missing receive watermark → RejectedNotReady; missing evaluation stamp → MissingEvaluationEventTime. Both fail closed. No unwraps on missing data: the two .expect() in classify_realized_vol_snapshot fire only on the post-Accepted path the gate already guaranteed (snapshot present + watermark present). No invented timestamps, no fallback clocks in production (signal/book paths use ts_init; selection uses local handler now_ms).
- Accepted-only causal watermarks: compute_rv derives latest_used_receive_ms as max over base grid ∪ noise estimator grid (coarse/subsampled lanes); snapshot takes max over ready quorum sources. Trimmed/quantile-unselected sources remain causal contributors (they affect quorum/dispersion). Rejected observations advance neither event nor receive watermark. Proven by 6 dedicated differentials.
Objective 2 — Entry/maker/exit ownership: SOUND
- Entry freezes one EntryRealizedVolatilityReceipt at evaluation (entry_realized_volatility_receipt_at). Logs, skip evidence, and submit-linked strategy-input evidence all consume decision.evaluation.realized_volatility_receipt — none re-query mutable RV state. submit_admitted_entry_decision cannot veto by re-gating. Proven by snapshot-replacement differentials (admit A, replace with B, assert A retained across log/skip/submit).
- Maker uses explicit caller-owned evaluation_receive_ms; the selected quote receive stamp describes only that input. No maker production caller added (input struct constructed only in tests; maker strategy holds it as a typed field).
- Exit dedup cannot oscillate: 6 alternating-venue-clock book evaluations → 1 record; 4 mixed book/signal/selection triggers → identical (exit_decision, blocked_reason, hold_ev_bps) + 1 record. Diagnostic future-dating delta now keyed off diagnostic_event_ms (venue), evidence/diagnostic only.
Objective 3 — Compile-fix commit ef48cb750: CORRECT
- Three optional diagnostic fields (fair_probability_up/down, uncertainty_band_probability) use fail-closed .and_then(|receive_ms| …) propagation (mod.rs:5816).
- Log-capture helpers consolidated to ONE implementation in shared_fixture.rs (pub(super)), correct visibility for sibling test submodules; synchronization (OnceLock install, Mutex observer guard, reset/capture) unchanged. with_captured_error_log delegates and retains len==1 + Error severity assertions; net filter (failure_message ∧ strategy_id) identical.
- Only the four obsolete Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(...)) wrappers on current_surfaced_realized_vol_snapshot_at (now takes required LocalReceiveMs) removed; classify_rv_gate diagnostic calls correctly retain Some(...).

   • Severity: Critical / merge blocker

         conclusion failure

           • error[E0689]: can't call method saturating_mulon ambiguous numeric
             type{integer}atsrc/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/tests/pricing.rs:433:26`
           • error[E0689]: can't call method saturating_mulon ambiguous numeric
             type{integer}atsrc/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/tests/source_evidence.rs:2908:26`
   • Issue: Two new tests declare let lifecycle_now_ms = 1_200; and then call
     lifecycle_now_ms.saturating_mul(NANOS_PER_MILLI_U64). Without an explicit type annotation the literal
     is an ambiguous integer, so cargo test --no-run fails.
   • Consequence: No test archive is produced and the root gate cannot run, so none of the previously
     failing tests can be verified by exact-head CI.
   • Required correction: Add : u64 type annotations to both lifecycle_now_ms bindings (pricing.rs:429,
     source_evidence.rs:2903), re-push, and re-run exact-head root CI until gate is green.
   • Scope ruling: In scope — introduced by the new signal-exit and book-route test additions in this PR.

   Non-blocking findings

   2. expect() on receive time in ready-source path remains

   • Severity: Low
   • Evidence: src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:539-540: latest_used_receive_ms.expect("a ready source
     must contain an accepted sample").
   • Issue: The panic is unreachable today, but the invariant is hand-maintained across functions rather
     than enforced by types.
   • Consequence: A future refactor could break the invariant and panic the hot pricing path.
   • Required correction: Restructure so the ready path returns a non-Option LocalReceiveMs (or a dedicated
     ready struct), eliminating the expect.
   • Scope ruling: In scope — prior finding not yet addressed.

   • Evidence: Current PR body Scope bullet reads only: “The separate reference-price clock-domain

     (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-rv-gate-clock-domain.md:48-53) explicitly disclose that the defect
     fails closed today.
   • Issue: A reviewer reading only the PR body may not know the excluded defect currently fails closed.
   • Consequence: Minor documentation incompleteness; no behavior impact.
   • Required correction: Add “and currently fails closed” to the Scope bullet.
   • Scope ruling: In scope — prior documentation finding not yet addressed.

      through the deserializer.

Kimi: Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES

   One substantive plan correction is required before implementation can proceed safely. No other
   substantive findings.

   Finding 1 — Durable/wire field type for rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms

   • Evidence:
       • LocalReceiveMs is defined at src/bolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs:34 with only Debug, Clone, Copy,
         PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord; it does not derive Serialize/Deserialize.
       • The durable exit evidence structs (BoltV3ExitDecisionEvidence at
         src/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:894 and BoltV3ExitEvaluationEvidence at :1663) derive Serialize
         and store every other timestamp as bare u64 / Option<u64>.
       • The plan states: “Add rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms: Option<LocalReceiveMs> and
         rv_max_source_age_ms: Option<u64> to both durable structs and optional u64 fields to both wire
         structs.”

   • Consequence: Implemented literally, adding Option<LocalReceiveMs> to a Serialize-derived evidence
     struct will not compile unless Serialize is also added to LocalReceiveMs. If that is done ad-hoc, the
     wire shape could diverge from the existing integer-timestamp convention and from the schema document
     unless the serialization shape is explicitly verified.

   • Required correction: State unambiguously that the durable evidence field stores Option<u64>
     (milliseconds), populated from receipt.watermark.value() or equivalent, and that the wire struct uses
     Option<u64>. Keep the schema document (docs/bolt-v3/2026-04-25-bolt-v3-schema.md) aligned with integer
     semantics. If LocalReceiveMs is intentionally stored in the durable struct, add Serialize/Deserialize
     to it and prove in the wire tests that it serializes as a bare integer (not an object/array).
