REQUEST CHANGES

Reviewer: me (Claude Code main loop), model claude-opus-4-8[1m] (Opus 4.8, 1M context). Reasoning-effort setting is not exposed to me, so I can't report it — I'm flagging that rather than guessing. Two Explore subagents ran census work (evidence-literal blast radius; policy-lookup call graph); I personally re-read every anchor below at the review head with git show/git grep and treated their reports as drafts only.

Reviewed: base f3555d49468a0326c917bdda91612d71da136b34 → head 3149d6a2e5f850e9c2b0bfb3765b0e106c62e465 (branch fix/1354-rv-gate-clock-domain-fresh; the single commit docs(#1354): specify RV gate evidence follow-up, docs-only, +251/−17 across the two named files). Working tree clean, on main @ 52a04f8546d97cd024c5d614e379b37a99c0c970; I read the review head out of git and never checked it out. The base is not an ancestor of origin/main. Read-only throughout: no edits, no commits, no CI, no GitHub mutation.

The amendment is well-aimed — it targets real defects I confirmed in the code, and its two hardest structural claims (capture-before-early-return, and the existing episode-end reset sites) both check out. But four issues block it.

---
Blocking
stamp, snapshot presence/readiness projection, receive watermark and maximum age" (design line 194-195) — an input list whose natural reading puts readiness before watermark and age.

Failure scenario: a snapshot with ready == true, a non-empty blocked_reasons, an accepted watermark, and an age past max_source_age_ms. The design itself names this shape ("raw-ready snapshots that have a blocker"), and it is representable because ready: bool and blocked_reasons are independent fields on RealizedVolSnapshot (src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:253-264). The real classifier returns RejectedStale (staleness fires first). A recomputation written in the design's stated input order returns RejectedNotReady. The plan's rv_clock_domain_amendment_records_recompute_gate_from_owned_inputs then either fails, or gets "fixed" by asserting the wrong expected value.

Required correction: state the precedence explicitly in the design as the normative recomputation algorithm, and extend the plan's test table beyond "all six categories" to the combinations where order discriminates: not-ready ∧ stale, not-ready ∧ future-dated, blocker ∧ stale. A six-row table cannot detect this class.

Scope: in scope — it is the correctness of the very reconstruction this amendment exists to deliver.

B2. Decision evidence's realized_vol is gate-filtered, so the design's stated equivalence is false and the Accepted arm of the reconstruction is circular

The design asserts (line 195-198) that decision evidence's usable-readiness projection is "raw rv_snapshot_ready && rv_snapshot_blockers.is_empty() plus a present realized_vol string that parses to a finite value greater than or equal to zero, matching ready_realized_vol()."

It does not match. ExitEvaluationLogFields.realized_vol is populated from current_realized_vol_for_gate_at (mod.rs:5813-5814) → current_realized_vol_at → current_surfaced_realized_vol_at → current_surfaced_realized_vol_snapshot_at (bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:416-438), which returns the snapshot only when the gate is Accepted. So durable realized_vol is Some iff the stored gate is Accepted — not iff ready_realized_vol().is_some(). A ready snapshot carrying RV = 1.5 that is RejectedStale has ready_realized_vol() == Some(1.5) but records realized_vol: null.

Two consequences. First, the final Accepted-vs-RejectedNotReady discrimination in the reconstruction reads a field that is itself a function of the stored gate, so that arm of the test proves nothing — it is tautological, which is exactly the false confidence the amendment is supposed to eliminate. Second, and worse: an implementer who notices the equivalence is false and "fixes" it by sourcing realized_vol from the receipt's raw snapshot value (the design does say the receipt owns the snapshot "value") would silently change the meaning of an existing schema-15 durable field — from "the RV actually used for pricing" to "the raw RV present even on a rejected sstructs." That will not compile. src/bolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs is not in Task 7's file list, so the implementer would have to either silently deviate from the stated type or silently promote a timestamp-domain newtype into the wire format — a design decision this document does not authorize.

Related, in the same sentence: the plan says "optional u64 fields to both wire structs," but the evaluation record's sibling timestamps are all i64 (trigger_ts_init_ms: Option<i64>, rv_as_of_ms: Option<i64> — :1798-1805), while the decision record's are u64. A u64 watermark next to an i64 trigger stamp in the same record forces the recomputation to compare across signedness.

Required correction: store Option<u64> in the decision record and Option<i64> in the evaluation record, matching each record's existing convention and keeping the newtype off the wire (recommended); or add src/bolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs to the file list with an explicit ruling that LocalReceiveMs becomes a serialized type.

Scope: in scope — it is the stated shape of the two fields this amendment adds.

B4. The policy lookup being replaced also feeds entry, and both of its owners are missing from the file list — leaving a second, fail-open home for the same policy

BinaryOracleEdgeTaker::realized_volatility_max_source_age_ms() (mod.rs:3628-3633) is the single wrapper over StrategyBuildContext::realized_volatility_max_source_age_ms_for_surface (src/strategies/registry.rs:265-273) → RealizedVolSurfaceRuntime::max_source_age_ms_for_surface (src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility_runtime.rs:195-199), which returns None on a plain map miss. None disables the staleness check entirely — classify_rv_gate guards it with max_source_age_ms.is_some_and(...) (bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:520). That is the fail-open the design is right to eliminate.

But its six production call sites are not exit-only:

- Entry: mod.rs:3637 (runtime_taker_pricing_config, which fans out to entry pricing inputs, entry fair probability, entry uncertainty band, fee-uncertainty adjustment, and scaled min edge) and mod.rs:6270 (entry_realized_volatility_receipt_at).
- Exit: mod.rs:5739, mod.rs:6934, mod.rs:3717.
- Shared entry+exit: mod.rs:3707 (current_realized_vol_for_gate_at).

Because mod.rs:3707 is shared, an exit-only rewire is not even possible — the implementer is forced to change the wrapper, which changes entry too. That is the right outcome, but it means registry.rs:265 and realized_volatility_runtime.rs:195 go dead, and neither file is in Task 7's file list, so the fail-open accessor survives as a second home for a policy the design says is "required and … captured at startup, never recovered inside an exit evaluation."
M1. "Fails startup rather than an exit callback" is only implementable at raw_taker_config; the plan also demands it at a layer that structurally cannot do it

validate_strategies (src/bolt_v3_validate.rs:212, surface cross-reference at :254-265) has exactly two production callers — src/bolt_v3_config.rs:1221 and :1369, both file-load paths. It does not run on BinaryOracleEdgeTakerBuilder::parse_config, whose signature is parse_config(raw: &Value) -> Result<BinaryOracleEdgeTakerConfig> (config.rs:272): it receives a bare TOML table and has no access to loaded.root.realized_volatility_surfaces, so it cannot resolve a surface id at all.

Plan Step 4 nonetheless requires "fail the existing binding/startup Result path if direct/post-load construction supplies an unknown surface." Not implementable there. The only enforcement parse_config can offer is that the copied scalar is present and positive.

Also, loaded.root.realized_volatility_surfaces is itself Option<BTreeMap<…>> (bolt_v3_config.rs:84), so there are two distinct failure shapes — the surfaces block absent entirely, versus the id absent from it — and the design names only one.

Required correction: assign surface resolution and its failure exclusively to raw_taker_config (src/bolt_v3_archetypes/binary_oracle_edge_taker.rs:661, called from register_runtime_strategy at :450 — it already has loaded.root and already returns Result<Value, BinaryOracleEdgeTakerRuntimeConfigError>), say that parse_config enforces present-and-positive only, and cover both failure shapes.

M2. The episode bound is per-episode, and the episode count is unbounded because the promoted "stable base key" retains tick-varying RV diagnostics

The design claims "The semantic set is bounded by the closed six-category gate enum times two watermark presence values." That bounds records per episode, and an episode ends on base-key change.

But BlockedStrategyInputDedupeKey (entry_decision.rs:339-360), which the design promotes verbatim to BlockedStrategyInputDedupeBaseKey, already carries realized_volatility_blockers: Vec<String>, realized_volatility_source_states: Vec<BlockedStrategyInputSourceStateKey> — each with status, block_reason, last_rejected_reason — and realized_volatility_unknown_source_ids. These are per-source RV diagnostics that churn under precisely the conditions the flood guard exists for. Every churn mints a new base key, hence a new episode, hence the whole semantic set may emit again. The plan's tests hold the base key fixed, so they cannot see this.

In the same passage the design asserts "Raw watermark values, timestamps, ages, prices, counters and volatility values remain outside both base keys and semantic-state keys." That is false about the base keys as written: EntrySkipDedupeKey.interval_open: O
Against a tests-only commit, ExitRealizedVolatilityGateReceipt, both evidence fields, and the config field do not exist, so the crate's test binaries fail to build. nextest never runs a test; the name filter is inert. The RED proves only "this does not compile" — it cannot distinguish a test that asserts the right thing from a test that asserts nothing, which is the one property this RED needs to establish (that the new tests fail against the old behavior).

Required correction, inside the same one-command budget: land the declarations as behavior-preserving stubs first (receipt built by re-querying current state; the two evidence fields written as None), so the tests compile and the single authorized run yields assertion failures naming wrong values. Then implement. This satisfies both the local-Rust constraint and differential-test discipline.

Minor

m1. The "cannot diverge" claim is broader than the receipt. current_position_fair_probability_up_for_gate_at (mod.rs:5361-5378) depends on spot_price (via mutable pricing.selected_pricing_spot()), strike_price, seconds_to_expiry(now_ms) and RV. The receipt freezes the resulting probabilities but not spot_price, which ExitEvaluationLogFields still reads at record time (mod.rs:5803). So a record's fair_probability_up need not be reproducible from its own recorded spot_price/interval_open/realized_vol. This is currently unreachable in production (decision and record run in one callback with no interleaved market data), but the plan's replacement test only swaps the RV snapshot and would not catch a spot swap. Scope the claim to RV-derived fields and state the single-callback atomicity argument that makes the rest safe.

m2. "Unmeasured" conflates a cheap bound with a real unknown. The two new keys add a fixed ~60–70 bytes per exit record, and the design's own counterfactual is 106 exit records / 199,023 bytes — so the exit-record increase is deterministically boundable at roughly four orders of magnitude below the 1 MiB fail-closed ceiling. What is genuinely unmeasured is the entry record-count increase from semantic dedupe (M2). State the exit bound arithmetically and confine "unmeasured" to where it is true.

m3. classify_rv_gate vs classify_realized_vol_gate are used interchangeably in prose but are different symbols — the free function (bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:503) versus the TakerPricingState method that wraps it (bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs:364). The design's Root Cause and Verification Contract say one; the new section and plan Task 8 say the other. Pin which symbol each sentence means. (Checked and clean: the source fence's FORBIDDEN_RAW_RV_CONSUMER_PATTERNS — tests/bolt_v3_realized_volatility_source_fence.rs:46-52 — forbids the old classify_realized_vol_gate(&self.config.realized_volatility_surface_id, now_ms) shape, and nothing in the amendment reintroduces it.)
as_of.signed_delta_since(trigger_event_ms) (mod.rs:6938-6942), and rv_future_dating_delta_ms is its positive component (mod.rs:5753-5756). The field name is a pre-existing misnomer — it is minus-trigger-event, not minus-now — and the design correctly preserves the semantics rather than the name. Worth one sentence in the doc so the next reader isn't misled.

The post-capture recomputation the amendment deletes is real. record_exit_evaluation_evidence re-runs the classifier at mod.rs:6931 after exit_evaluation_log_fields_at already ran it at :5736; it re-reads the current snapshot for rv_ready at :6943; and fair_probability_up is computed twice at :5830-5839. The finding this amendment closes is genuine.

Six gate categories, and BTreeSet will work. BoltV3RvGateResult (bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:1698-1707) has exactly the six named variants and already derives Ord + Hash.

The episode-end reset sites exist and mean what the plan says. last_recorded_blocked_strategy_input = None at mod.rs:7210 is the else-branch of the RV-not-ready test; last_recorded_entry_skip = None at mod.rs:7267 fires on admitted entry. Writer semantics also check out: entry-skip swallows its write error (mod.rs:4329-4343) and marks unconditionally at :4344; blocked-snapshot propagates via ? (:6498) and marks only after success (:6499). The plan's "preserve existing" framing here is accurate, not aspirational.

Wire/schema compatibility holds, and the plan's census is right. Schema version is 15 (bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:38). Adding two Option fields breaks exactly eight struct literals — four production (bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:1072, :1833, exit_decision.rs:264, mod.rs:6987) and four test (bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:5139, tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:1149, :1455, :1492) — so the plan's "all three integration struct literals" is exactly correct. Both predeploy fixtures hold one record each, neither carries the new keys, and neither needs editing if the fields are Option. There is no exact-key-set or exact-serialized-bytes assertion on either exit record type, so no silent schema break. docs/bolt-v3/2026-04-25-bolt-v3-schema.md:662 is the one field enumeration to extend. And tests/bolt_v3_strategy_registration.rs earns its place in the file list — it has ~25 raw_taker_config call sites that break when a required config field is added. No schema bump is needed.

Scope and honesty hold. The reference-price clock-domain defect is correctly kept separate and gated on explicit approval; #1275 item 13, #883, and #763 are correctly sequenced and not bundled; the archive arithmetic and the 1 MiB boundary are unchanged; the PR-body head-agnostic rule is preserved.

Findings

F1 — BLOCKING. "Propagate conversion failure through the writer; never … drop" is not implementable, and the only implementable reading regresses an exit-path safety invariant

Evidence. record_exit_evaluation_eviden
 conversion failure "propagates through the existing evidence write/error path and never wraps, saturates, or becomes None", and plan Step 4 (line 403-404) says "Propagate conversion failure through the writer; never cast/saturate/drop."

Consequence. An implementer has exactly two options and the plan forbids both:

- Skip the write and log — i.e. drop the record. Forbidden verbatim by "never … drop".
- Change the signature to Result and ? the conversion. That makes an evidence-layer conversion failure abort the exit callback. The sibling entry-skip path carries an explicit invariant against precisely this at mod.rs:4334-4338: "a telemetry-write failure must never abort the strategy callback (which would skip downstream safety logic such as enforce_one_position_invariant)." The design's own forced-flat-availability requirement says the same thing for exits.

The RED test the plan schedules ("Add u64::MAX conversion tests … require the existing error path", Step 1
line 312-314) therefore asserts againstt.

Required correction. Pick one and state it. i64::try_from on a u64 millisecond stamp can only fail above i64::MAX (~2.9×10⁸ years), so the pragmatic answer is fail-loud-and-skip: log at error, do not write the record, and delete "never … drop" from Step 4 and the Interfaces block. If propagation is genuinely wanted, change the signature to Result and explicitly rule on — and test — the callback-abort question against mod.rs:4334-4338. Either way the RED assertion must name the chosen observable behavior.

Scope. In scope: it is the stated behavior of a field this amendment adds and of an existing cast it changes.

  ## Verified facts

  - Replay order exactly matches source-base classify_rv_gate: missing snapshot → missing evaluation receive
    → missing watermark → future → stale → unusable readiness → accepted.

    watermark inputs.

    rejection.

  - Entry dedupe uses a 12-bit category×presence mask with one current existing key, no key history, raw-
    watermark suppression, presence discrimination, and atomic blocked-writer state updates.

    matches, and setup failures are rejected. Its mask is test-local.

    Subsequent source/document changes are prohibited.

    15,158; fixed-record total remains below 1 MiB.

  - Semantic-entry record growth remains explicitly unmeasured. #1275 item 13, reference-price correction,
    #883, and #763 remain excluded.

   Findings

   1. Config-field SSOT macro is not explicitly named in the plan

   • Severity: Low (will fail compile/tests if missed).
   • Evidence: BinaryOracleEdgeTakerConfig and the validate_table allowlist are both generated from the

   • Severity: Low.

     StrategyBuildContext::realized_volatility_max_source_age_ms_for_surface. The plan deletes the obsolete

     deleting this wrapper.

   Question-by-question verdict

   1. Replay correctness — Yes. The documented precedence (MissingSnapshot → MissingEvaluationEventTime →
      missing watermark → RejectedNotReady → FutureDated → Stale → unusable readiness → Accepted) matches
      the free classify_rv_gate at src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:503, and the wrapper at
      src/bolt_v3_taker_pricing.rs:364 delegates to it without redefining order. Future/stale are checked
      before readiness. Marker fields (rv_max_source_age_ms positive, decision

      legitimate None classifier inputs.

      usable-readiness boolean and of the gate-filtered realized_vol. Durable/wire types follow existing
      conventions. Evaluation trigger_ts_init_ms and rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms use i64::try_from

      identified caller: the three runtime_taker_pricing_config callers and the five direct policy
      consumers.

   4. Entry dedupe — Yes. The 12-bit {gate_result × watermark_presence} mask closes discussion_r3571669050.
      The existing dedupe key is preserved byte-for-byte; raw watermark values, timestamps, ages, prices,
      counters, and volatility values stay outside the novelty state. Blocked-snapshot key/mask commit only
      after a successful write; entry-skip marks before its swallowed-error write. The plan explicitly
      disclaims bounding unrelated existing-key churn or total evidence volume.

      behavioral assertions, never because a future field/type is undefined. The required test-local

Findings

N1 — MINOR. The receipt's blocker representation is under-specified, and the design removes the only current source of the evaluation record's rv_blockers without naming a replacement

Evidence. The two exit records derive their blockers from the same upstream RealizedVolSnapshot.blocked_reasons: Vec<RealizedVolBlockReason> (bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:266) through two different label spaces:

- Decision → Vec<BoltV3ExitRvSnapshotBlocker> via realized_vol_blocker_to_exit_evidence (mod.rs:5741-5746); the enum has 8 variants (decision_evidence.rs:919-927).
- Evaluation → Vec<String> via realizedvidence_label(...).to_string(), reached
through RealizedVolatilityEvidenceField.rs:118; built at mod.rs:6233-6239;consumed at mod.rs:6999).

The design (line 180-185) says the receipt owns "mapped exit blockers/diagnostics" and "must not own … the full entry-oriented RealizedVolatilityEvidenceFields" — which is the evaluation record's only current source for rv_blockers. No replacement is named.

Note the diagnostics half is fine and IatilityEvidenceFields.source_diagnostics

Consequence. The implementer must invent a representation. The natural reading of "mapped exit blockers" is Vec<BoltV3ExitRvSnapshotBlocker> — from which the evaluation record's Vec<String> is not derivable without a new enum→label mapping. Any drift between that new mapping and realized_volatility_block_reason_evidence_label silently changes the content of an existing schema-15 durable field. That is precisely the class of silent semantic change this amendment exists to prevent, and the plan's stated tests would not catch it: the immutability test (Step 1, line 326) asserts blockers "remain the RV source" after snapshot replacement, which proves immutability, not content-equivalence with today's records.

Required correction. One sentence: the receipt owns the raw Vec<RealizedVolBlockReason> (a Copy enum vec — strictly more compact than either projection), and each durable builder applies its existing mapping unchanged. Alternatively, state that the receipt owns both projections. Either way, name it, and add an
assertion that the evaluation record's te-identical to today's.

Scope. In scope — it is an enumerated field of the receipt this amendment introduces.

N2 — MINOR (advisory). The structural-ad residual hit the plan does not name

Evidence. Step 6 (line 535) audits withne\(\)'src/strategies/binary_oracle_edge_taker/. There are two evaluation: evaluation.clone() sites: mod.rs:5623 (exit — exit_submission_decision_from_evaluation, the one to remove) and mod.rs:7057 (entry — inside entry_submission_decision_for_receive_at, mod.rs:7050, which is out of scope).
implies the right answer but never says the entry hit is expected.

Required correction. Add: the entry-side evaluation.clone() at entry_submission_decision_for_receive_at remains and is out of scope; only the ExitSubmissionDecision construction must show a move.

Scope. In scope, doc-only.

(Feasibility of the move, verified: exit_submission_decision_from_evaluation(&self, evaluation: ExitEvaluation) already takes the value by owner, so the .clone() at mod.rs:5623 is gratuitous — but evaluation is read again at mod.rs:5642, 5643, 5646, 5650, 5670, so the move requires a small reorder, not just deleting .clone(). Straightforward; worth the plan saying so.)

   1. Evidence-build failure — Closed.

         prohibited.

         are explicitly out of scope.

       • Pre-run work is limited to static inspection; compile proof is not claimed before that invocation.
       • Compiler error, zero matches, setup/harness failure, or interruption invalidates RED and requires
         explicit owner approval before any rerun/retry/check/no-run/probe/second invocation.
       • The historical Tasks 1–4 RED prohibition is explicitly carved out for the new Task 7 invocation.

   5. Config/policy ownership — Closed.
       • The config macro is named as the SSOT for the derived field and allowlist.
       • Root validate_realized_volatility_surfaces already rejects zero; the new parse_config zero
         rejection is described as defense-in-depth for bypass callers.

         runtime_taker_pricing_config are all explicitly deleted; every identified caller is rewired.

         RealizedVolatilityEvidenceFields.

   • The correction replaces wrapping timestamp arithmetic and deep evaluation clones with checked
     conversions and moves, eliminating the prior safety/performance concerns without changing trading
     semantics.

     schema-owned projections without owning the full snapshot or classification, and ExitEvaluation is
     already consumed by value in exit_submission_decision_from_evaluation.

RULING: APPROVE

Both N1 and N2 are closed, and the closures are better than what I asked for. No regressions in the surrounding contracts. No remaining findings.

---
N1 — blocker representation: CLOSED

Every element checks out against source:

- Receipt owns the ordered raw vector. Design line 181-183 and plan Task-7 Interfaces now say the receipt owns "the raw snapshot blockers as an ordered Vec<RealizedVolBlockReason>," and both docs add "Do not pre-map/reorder blockers or introduce mapping semantics." RealizedVolBlockReason derives Copy (bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:389), so the vector is a cheap copy — genuinely more compact than owning either projection, which is what makes this the right call rather than merely an adequate one.
- Both builders keep their existing mapping functions, unchanged. Design 188-193 assigns realized_vol_blocker_to_exit_evidence to the decision builder and realized_volatility_block_reason_evidence_label(...).to_string() to the evaluation builder, independently. I verified both exist (bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:516 and :494) and that both are total and 1:1 over all ten variants — InvalidConfig, QuorumNotReady, SourceStale, CoverageBelowMinimum, InterSampleGapExceeded, SourceClassMismatch, SampleKindMismatch, CrossSourceDispersion, AnnualizationBasisInvalid, NotWarm. (My prior review said BoltV3ExitRvSnapshotBlocker had only eight variants; that was a truncated grep on my part. It has all ten, and the projection is lossless. Correcting my own record.)
- "No new blocker mapping semantics are introduced" is stated explicitly in both docs.
- Tests pin pre-amendment output on both sides. Plan Step 1 now requires exercising "every current RealizedVolBlockReason in source order" and pinning "the exact current pre-amendment decision blocker enum vector and evaluation string-label vector as direct expected record outputs," with "element/byte identity before and after replacement." The design's Verification Contract carries the same requirement. This is precisely the content-equivalence assertion the old immutability-only test could not make — it closes the silent-label-drift path I flagged.

Two things I checked that could have gone wrong and did not:

RealizedVolBlockReason::ALL exists as a pub const &'static [Self] (bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:404), so "every current variant in source order" is mechanically enumerable rather than a hand-copied list. A future variant cannot be silently missed by the test.

No source-fence conflict. The strategy fence forbids the literal term CrossSourceDispersion in all src/strategies/** production sources (tests/bolt_v3_realized_volatility_source_fence.rs:3-12), and that is a RealizedVolBlockReason variant. But holding Vec<RealizedVolBlockReason> never names a variant — the receipt declares only the type, and both mappings are called as functions defined in bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs (which the fence does not scan for strategy terms). This is exactly what the current code already does at mod.rs:5741-5746 and :6233-6239, and the fence passes today. Tests that enumerate variants live under tests/ directories, which strategy_production_sources skips (source_fence.rs:158-165).

The diagnostics half needs no equivalent pinning and I confirmed why: RealizedVolatilityEvidenceFields.source_diagnostics is already Vec<BoltV3RealizedVolatilitySourceDiagnosticEvidence> (entry_decision.rs:116) — the identical type the decision record's schema owns — and that struct carries source_id and status as String (decision_evidence.rs:401, 406), so the evaluation record's format!("{source_id}:{status}") derives from it losslessly. One receipt field serves both records with no mapping ambiguity to resolve.

N2 — clone audit: CLOSED

- Only the exit-side clone is removed. Design 195-201 and plan Step 4 name exit_submission_decision_from_evaluation specifically and require that "values that function still needs are computed/cached or read before ExitEvaluation is moved," with behavior unchanged. That matches the source: the .clone() at mod.rs:5623 is gratuitous (the parameter is already taken by value) but evaluation is read again at :5642, 5643, 5646, 5650, 5670, so the fix is a reorder, not a deletion — and the plan now says so.
- The entry-side clone is explicitly expected and out of scope. Both docs name entry_submission_decision_for_receive_at (the clone at mod.rs:7057) as remaining.
- The audit classifies rather than requiring zero matches. The Step 6 regex now includes both function names, and the instruction reads: "A directory-wide hit for the existing entry_submission_decision_for_receive_at evaluation.clone() is expected and out of scope; classify it explicitly rather than treating the search as clean. Fail the audit only if the exit capture/submission path still clones the evaluation, and verify its later reads were cached or reordered before the move without changing behavior." The false-failure trap is gone, and so is the risk of an auditor "fixing" the entry path.

Regression check — all prior contracts intact

The range is a single commit touching four passages (receipt projection, blocker mapping, clone scoping, audit regex) plus one Verification Contract bullet. I confirmed at head 0b4d16222 that nothing else moved:

- Replay — the seven-step normative precedence still mirrors classify_rv_gate (bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:508-528) exactly, and snapshot presence is still named as rv_snapshot_as_of_ms.is_some() / rv_as_of_ms.is_some().
- Conversions — all five outbound evaluation absolute times still use i64::try_from; the delta is still computed losslessly as i128::from(snapshot_as_of_ms) - i128::from(trigger_event_ms) with signed_delta_since and as i64 still banned; checked narrowing on both projections.
- Failure — design 268-274 still has record_exit_evaluation_evidence unit-returning and non-aborting, private Result helper after the existing dedupe mark, one log::error!, no partial record, no substituted value, trading result preserved, mark-before-failure retained. Fail-loud-and-skip, not propagation into trading.
- RED budget — the single nextest invocation is still both compilation proof and behavioral-RED proof, with stop-and-ask on compile error, zero matches, setup failure, or interruption.
- Config/policy — macro SSOT (binary_oracle_edge_taker_config_fields!), the accurate description of root's existing "must be a positive integer" check, and "zero definitions or calls" for all three deleted policy wrappers all survive.
- Dedupe — {current_existing_key, rv_seen_mask: u16}, twelve bits, key-change clears mask, atomic blocked-writer staging.
- Capacity — 143 × 106 = 15,158 bytes, semantic-entry growth still explicitly unmeasured.
- Final plan removal — still ordered before the final census, internal review, and publication.

(Two of my regression greps returned zero on first pass; both were line-wrapping artifacts in the prose, not missing text. I re-read both passages in full.)

   Non-blocking findings

   1. New production expect panic paths rely on mechanically guaranteed invariants

   • Severity: low
   • Evidence:
       • src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:232-241 (classify_realized_vol_snapshot):
         ```rust
           let snapshot = snapshot
               .expect("accepted RV classification mechanically requires a snapshot")
               .clone();
           let receive_watermark_ms = snapshot
               .latest_accepted_receive_ms
               .expect("accepted RV classification mechanically requires a receive watermark");
           let ready_realized_vol = snapshot
               .ready_realized_vol()
               .expect("accepted RV classification mechanically requires a ready value")
               .get();
         ```
       • src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:538-540 (RealizedVolEngine::snapshot_at):
         ```rust
           latest_accepted_receive_ms: latest_used_receive_ms
               .expect("a ready source must contain an accepted sample"),
         ```
   • Issue: These expects are new production panic paths. They are true today because classify_rv_gate returning Accepted implies a snapshot, a receive watermark, and a
     ready value, and because a ready source's compute_rv always populates latest_used_receive_ms. A future regression in any of those invariants would abort the
     strategy callback instead of failing closed.
   • Concrete consequence: A drift in classify_rv_gate, RealizedVolSnapshot::ready, ready_realized_vol(), or compute_rv turns a latent classification bug into a strategy
     crash.
   • Required correction: Replace the expects with explicit Rejected/None or Result handling so the failure mode stays record-local and non-aborting. For
     classify_realized_vol_snapshot, return a Rejected classification; for snapshot_at, omit the value or return a diagnostic classification.
   • Scope ruling: Within the #1377 amendment. Not a merge blocker because the invariants are currently enforced by the same code that contains the expects, and the
     surrounding failure mode (evidence-build skip) is otherwise consistent.

   Note: unchanged VenueEventMs::signed_delta_since still uses as i64

   src/bolt_v3_timestamp_domain.rs:28-30 still implements signed_delta_since with self.0 as i64 - baseline.0 as i64. This is not a new finding for #1377: the function is
   unchanged and is not called by any new clock-domain path in this diff. The new code computes the signed decision/evaluation deltas losslessly as i128 and narrows with
   checked try_from.

FINAL EXTERNAL ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — PR #1377

I am revising the ruling I gave earlier in this session. I issued a preliminary APPROVE before my delegated test reviews returned. They returned with two substantive findings that I have now independently confirmed against source. My earlier ruling was premature and I withdraw it.

---
BLOCKING FINDINGS

B1 — HIGH. The replay ladder is never bound to production's classifier, and production's precedence is unpinned. The closure evidence for PR finding #1 is incomplete.

- Evidence. classify_rv_gate (src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:503-529) has zero callers in tests/ (git grep classify_rv_gate 1666e8c5e -- tests/ → no hits). The replay ladder rv_clock_domain_amendment_replayed_gate (tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:2417-2465) is an independent hand-written reimplementation. The two are never compared.
Production's own precedence is likewise unlocked. Every inline test that reaches the future/stale arms (:572-583, :625-641) uses a ready snapshot, and the one "not ready" test (:583-593) uses not_ready_snapshot() = RealizedVolSnapshot::inva(tests/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:72 already does it). For each of the eight existing Cases, build the equivalent snapshot + LocalReceiveMs + max age and assert classify_rv_gate(...) == rv_clock_domain_amendment_replayed_gate(record). Add the missing (as_of == None && receive == None) case, which is the only one locking MissingSnapshot ahead of MissingEvaluationEventTime.
- Scope ruling. In scope, and squarely on the deliverable. The design doc asserts "Replay mirrors the free classify_rv_gate function exactly, in this normative precedence order" — that "exactly" is untested.

B2 — HIGH. "A key change resets the RV mask" is unproven; a mask-carrying mutant survives. The closure evidence for PR finding #2 is incomplete.

- Evidence. Production is correct: mod.rs:4314-4317 (and the twin at :6526-6529) reset via _ => EntrySkipDedupeState { current_key: key, rv_seen_mask: rv_bit }. But the _ arm emits unconditionally — it never consults the mask. So an emission on a key change proves nothing about the reset.
rv_clock_domain_amendment_existing_key_changes_reset_rv_mask (tests/source_evidence.rs:4903) calls rv_clock_domain_amendment_apply_state exactly once (:4907-4911, Accepted + Some(1_200)) and never varies the RV bit again — I confirmed this by counting: one call in the whole test body. All 17 emissions come from key-field mutations with the bit held constant. The blocked-path twin (:4803-4848) likewise fixes (RejectedNotReady, Some(1_200)) and asserts 3 records for A→B→A.
- Issue. Mutate the _ arm to carry the prior mask forward instead of resetting it, and every test still passes — because no test ever presents, after a key change, a bit that was already seen under the previous key.
- Consequence. The regression this guards against is exactly PR finding #2 (discussion_r3571669050: "entry evidence dedupe suppresses materially different RV gate diagnoses"). If the reset regressed, previously-seen gate states would stay suppressed across key changes — re-introducing the suppression the finding was raised to fix — and no test would notice.
- Required correction. Mark two bits under key A (e.g. Accepted+Some, then RejectedStale+Some), switch to key B, present Accepted+Some (emits either way), then present RejectedStale+Some and assert it emits (count 4, not 3). Same for the blocked path.
- Scope ruling. In scope; it is the acceptance test for one of the two findings this PR closes.

NON-BLOCKING FINDINGS

N1 — MEDIUM (tautology). tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:2657-2660: assert!(encoded.get("rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms").is_some(), "valid {label} watermark form must survive the durable round trip"). BoltV3ExitEvaluationEvidence derives Serialize with no skip_serializing_if on that field (src/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:1760-1774), so serde always emits the key and None becomes null — .get() returns Some(&Value::Null) for all four labels. The assertion cannot fail regardless of production behavior; if the encoder silently nulled the i64::MAX value it would still pass. The message claims value preservation; the assertion checks key presence. (The .expect(...) on from_value above it is meaningful.) Correction: assert_eq!(decoded.rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms, expected) per label. Verified by me.

N2 — MEDIUM (tautology). rv_clock_domain_amendment_exit_receipt_survives_early_returns (tests/source_evidence.rs:3712-3859) fabricates 7 of the 12 shapes by cloning a base evaluation and overwriting blocked_reason, rather than driving the strategy into them; and assert_eq!(decisions.len(), 12, "the fixture must cover all early returns") (:3793) asserts the length of a Vec the test itself pushed 12 items into. Add a 13th early return to production and the test still passes. The property is structurally guaranteed (the receipt is a non-Option field set at mod.rs:5598-5599 before any return — I verified this), so risk is low, but the test does not prove what its name claims. Agent-reported; I verified the structural guarantee and the self-counting assert, not the full shape enumeration.

N3 — MEDIUM. seed_ready_realized_vol (src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:458-461) sets as_of_ms: ready_ts_ms and latest_accepted_receive_ms: Some(LocalReceiveMs::new(ready_ts_ms)) — the venue-event stamp and the local-receive watermark are numerically identical in every snapshot built from it. The three headline receipt tests collapse as_of, watermark, ts_init, and now_ms all onto 1_200. Consequence: on the exit-decision record (all-u64, no checked conversion), substituting rv_snapshot_as_of_ms for rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms is currently unobservable. (Mitigations that do exist and are good: rv_clock_domain_amendment_exit_evaluation_conversion_failure_skips_record separates them via u64::MAX on the evaluation record, and signal_quote_exit_uses_pinned_quote_receive_stamp_without_fallback (:2918) is genuinely differential — now 1200 / event 301001 / receive 1220, where only ts_init yields Accepted.) Correction: give the fixture distinct as_of and receive stamps. Fixture fusion verified by me; the downstream collapse is agent-reported.

N4 — LOW. Legacy gate-result inference (src/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:881-890, :1570-1578) infers Accepted for pre-extension entry_skip / strategy_input_snapshot records. The inference is sound (those fields were populated only via the gate-filtered accessor, so their presence does imply the old gate accepted) and never invents a rejection category. But the acceptance it recovers was decided by the old event-domain comparison, while the enum is now defined by the receive-domain classifier. docs/bolt-v3/2026-04-25-bolt-v3-schema.md:655 discloses that an inference happens but not that it reflects the pre-fix gate. Correction: one sentence saying inferred Accepted must not be pooled with post-fix gate results. Verified by me.

N5 — LOW. rv_clock_domain_amendment_rv_bit (tests/source_evidence.rs:4295-4306) is a verbatim copy of production rv_gate_novelty_bit (entry_decision.rs:374-389), used to fold the expected mask. rv_gate_novelty_bit has no direct unit test pinning its 12 outputs to constants. Similarly rv_clock_domain_amendment_extreme_exit_deltas_are_lossless (:4234) recomputes i128::from(a) - i128::from(b) rather than hardcoding the expected values, so a saturating_sub mutant would pass. Agent-reported; not re-verified.

N6 — LOW. exit_evaluation_dedupe_does_not_oscillate_across_trigger_sources (:3386) and ..._ignores_alternating_consuming_venue_clock_lead (:3427) feed a constant minimal_exit_submission_decision() literal; ExitOutcomeKey (mod.rs:7060-7064) is built entirely from that constant, so records.len() == 1 holds regardless of the trigger clocks. The messages overclaim. Agent-reported; not re-verified.

N7 — LOW. The negativity fence covers 2 of 5 signed time fields — trigger_ts_init_ms and rv_snapshot_receive_watermark_ms (decision_evidence.rs:1846-1857, :3887-3894) — while exit_eval_now_ms, trigger_ts_event_ms, and rv_as_of_ms are equally provably non-negative at write time and unfenced. The design explicitly scoped this out ("negative inbound validation for the existing event/lifecycle/as-of fields is unchanged and out of scope"), so this is a declared decision, not a defect. Worth noting only because the replay ladder inherits the asymmetry: a corrupt rv_as_of_ms: -1 decodes cleanly and replays as snapshot present. Agent-reported; scope exclusion verified by me.

N8 — LOW. Six of the twelve mask bits are unreachable on the entry paths (Accepted+None, MissingSnapshot+Some, MissingEvaluationEventTime+{None,Some}, RejectedFutureDated+None, RejectedStale+None), because the entry receipt is built via classify_realized_vol_snapshot with a non-Option receive stamp and because Accepted/FutureDated/Stale all require a present watermark. Dead bits are harmless, but the 12-state tests reach them only by injecting receipts directly (apply_state, :4370-4380) — so no test drives the real gate through the dedupe path. Agent-reported; the unreachability argument follows from classify_rv_gate's structure, which I verified.

N9 — LOW. The blocked-snapshot writer-failure test exercises failure on a key change; the same-key, new-bit, write-fails branch (i.e. "a failed write must not mark the new bit as seen") is untested. Agent-reported; not re-verified.

FINAL ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — PR #1377

Blocking findings: none. Both prior blocking findings are closed.

B1 (prior) — Production/replay equivalence — CLOSED

classify_rv_gate is now imported (tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:45) and called from tests/ at :2880 and :3018 — previously zero callers there. The closure is genuine, not cosmetic:

- A real, self-validating snapshot fixture. rv_clock_domain_amendment_production_snapshot (:2402-2440) builds an actual RealizedVolSnapshot struct literal and guards itself three ways: an internal assert_eq!(usable_ready, raw_ready && !has_blockers, "fixture readiness must agree with the production usable-readiness contract") (:2409-2413), a post-construction assert_eq!(snapshot.ready_realized_vol().is_some(), usable_ready, "fixture must exercise production readiness rather than a stored gate result") (:2441-2445), and caller-side re-assertions of ready / ready_realized_vol() / latest_accepted_receive_ms / blocked_reasons (:2863-2877). The fixture cannot silently misrepresent a case.
- Both ladders pinned to the same hardcoded expectation. assert_eq!(production_result, case.expected, "{} must retain production classifier precedence") (:2889-2893) sits alongside the replay assertion against the identical case.expected.
- All ten required cases present (:2727-2848): missing_snapshot_and_evaluation → MissingSnapshot (locks the snapshot arm ahead of the receive arm), missing_snapshot, missing_evaluation, missing_watermark → RejectedNotReady, not_ready_plus_future (watermark 1201 > receive 1200, usable_ready: false) → RejectedFutureDated, not_ready_plus_stale (receive 1701, watermark 1200, usable_ready: false) → RejectedStale, blocker_plus_stale (raw_ready: true, blockers non-empty) → RejectedStale, ordinary not_ready, accepted_zero (all-zero timestamps), plus the above-i64::MAX case (:3011-3035) which builds a real snapshot at i64::MAX + 1 and asserts production returns RejectedStale and that decision replay equals high_production_result through i128.
- The stored gate is still poisoned. value["rv_gate_result"] = json!("accepted") in both the case loop (:2917) and the high-timestamp case (:2998), and the replay helper reads only rv_max_source_age_ms, rv_snapshot_has_ready_realized_vol / rv_ready (:2471-2478) — never the gate, never realized_vol.

The mutation now dies. Hoisting the readiness arm above future/stale makes not_ready_plus_future, not_ready_plus_stale, and blocker_plus_stale each return RejectedNotReady against hardcoded expectations of RejectedFutureDated/RejectedStale/RejectedStale — three failing asserts. Swapping the snapshot and receive arms fails missing_snapshot_and_evaluation. Removing the watermark arm fails missing_watermark.

B2 (prior) — RV mask reset across key changes — CLOSED

rv_clock_domain_amendment_key_change_resets_every_seen_rv_bit (source_evidence.rs:4598-4682) does exactly what was required, on both paths. It marks Accepted+Some(1_200) and RejectedStale+Some(1_200) under key A, changes key (entry: EntryPricingBlocked → NoSideSelected; blocked: market_id A → <KEY_B>), replays both bits, then returns to key A and replays both again — asserting the exact ordered six-record sequence assert_eq!(entry_states.as_slice(), &EXPECTED_STATES[..]) on recorded evidence, not internal state.

The mask-carrying mutant now dies. Under a mutant that ORs the prior mask into the _ arm: key A emits 2, key B emits only Accepted (the RejectedStale bit is carried and suppressed), key A again emits only Accepted — 4 records against an expected ordered 6. Because the assertion compares the exact (gate, watermark_present) sequence rather than a count, it also catches wrong-bit mapping.

Test count 61 → 64; the 59 deleted lines are all replacements (tautologies swapped for exact-value pins, messages corrected). No coverage was lost.

Concerns I assessed and withdrew (not defects — recorded so you can disagree)

- Partial negativity fence (2 of 5 signed fields). All five outbound absolute timestamps go through checked_timestamp (i64::try_from) at write, so none can be negative. Fencing only the two receive-domain fields this PR owns, and leaving the three pre-existing event/lifecycle/as-of fields alone, is the explicitly declared scope boundary in the design. The only exposure is an externally corrupted record — a property shared by every other i64 field in the schema. Not a defect.
- Six of twelve mask bits unreachable on entry paths. rv_gate_novelty_bit is a const fn with an exhaustive match over the closed six-variant enum, so a new gate variant is a compile error, not a silent collapse. Making the taxonomy deliberately total rather than pruning to the currently-reachable six is robust to the entry path later gaining a MissingEvaluationEventTime case, and costs one u16. Not a defect.
- The two historical "oscillation" tests feed a constant decision literal. Their ExitOutcomeKey is therefore constant and records.len() == 1 is guaranteed. But that is the point: they are intentionally isolated dedupe-key tests proving the outcome key collapses a flood, with the gate deliberately held fixed. The genuinely cross-trigger test exists separately. The messages are imprecise; the assertions are valid. Below the bar for a finding.
- Receipt captured before every exit early return. This is a mechanically enforced invariant, not a test obligation: realized_volatility_receipt is a non-Option field of ExitEvaluation set at construction (mod.rs:5598-5599) before all eight early returns. The compiler makes an unreceipted ExitEvaluation unconstructible. The honest rename (N2) is the correct response; no test could add assurance the type system doesn't already give.

• ## Blocking findings

  1. MEDIUM — Replay precedence proof remains incomplete
      - Evidence: At 9122781f:tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:2757-2797, the table covers:
          - absent snapshot + absent evaluation + absent watermark;
          - present snapshot + absent evaluation + present watermark;
          - present snapshot + present evaluation + absent watermark.

        It does not cover a present snapshot where both evaluation receive time and watermark are absent. Production checks missing evaluation before missing watermark at
        src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:511-515.

      - Issue: A mutant that swaps those two production checks would return RejectedNotReady instead of MissingEvaluationEventTime for that overlapping input, yet every
        new equivalence case would still pass.

      - Consequence: The tests correctly catch readiness being hoisted ahead of future/stale and catch snapshot-vs-evaluation precedence, but they do not prove the
        complete documented classifier precedence.

      - Required correction: Add a case with as_of_ms=Some(...), evaluation_receive_ms=None, and watermark_ms=None, expecting MissingEvaluationEventTime. Exercise it
        through public classify_rv_gate and both durable replay families while retaining the poisoned stored gate.

      - Scope ruling: In scope for #1354/#1377 and the focused correction. Test-only; no production change is required.

      marker) and the new tests replay the public classify_rv_gate from those inputs, with the stored rv_gate_result deliberately poisoned to Accepted so replay cannot

   Non-blocking findings

   1. New production expect panic paths remain unaddressed

   • Severity: low
   • Evidence:
       • src/bolt_v3_fair_value_pricing.rs:232-241 (classify_realized_vol_snapshot):
         ```rust
           let snapshot = snapshot
               .expect("accepted RV classification mechanically requires a snapshot")
               .clone();
           let receive_watermark_ms = snapshot
               .latest_accepted_receive_ms
               .expect("accepted RV classification mechanically requires a receive watermark");
           let ready_realized_vol = snapshot
               .ready_realized_vol()
               .expect("accepted RV classification mechanically requires a ready value")
               .get();
         ```
       • src/bolt_v3_realized_volatility.rs:538-540 (RealizedVolEngine::snapshot_at):
         ```rust
           latest_accepted_receive_ms: latest_used_receive_ms
               .expect("a ready source must contain an accepted sample"),
         ```
   • Issue: These are new production expect paths introduced by the PR. They hold today because classify_rv_gate returning Accepted implies a snapshot, a receive
     watermark, and a ready value, and compute_rv populates latest_used_receive_ms whenever it produces an RV. A future regression in any of those invariants would abort
     the strategy callback rather than fail closed.
   • Concrete consequence: A drift in classify_rv_gate, RealizedVolSnapshot::ready, ready_realized_vol(), or compute_rv turns a latent classification bug into a strategy
     crash.
   • Required correction: Replace the expects with explicit Rejected/None or Result handling so the failure mode stays record-local and non-aborting.
   • Scope ruling: Inside the #1377 amendment; not a merge blocker because the invariants are currently mechanically enforced by the same code, and the surrounding
     evidence-failure mode is otherwise record-local.

   2. No explicit adversarial test mutates the stored gate to a wrong non-Accepted value

   • Severity: low
   • Evidence: tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:2917 sets value["rv_gate_result"] = serde_json::json!("accepted") for every replay case. The replay helper
     rv_clock_domain_amendment_replayed_gate never reads rv_gate_result, so the test still proves replay ignores the stored gate.
   • Issue: The poison is always Accepted. A mutant that consults the stored gate would fail for most cases, but a future regression that accidentally falls back to the
     stored gate on some specific code path might not be caught if that stored gate happened to match.
   • Concrete consequence: Slightly weaker adversarial coverage than a parameterized wrong gate value (e.g., rejected_stale where production expects
     RejectedFutureDated).
   • Required correction: Optionally add one case that sets the stored gate to a deliberately wrong non-Accepted value and asserts replay still returns the production
     result.
   • Scope ruling: Test-coverage refinement only; the production contract is already correct.

FINAL ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — PR #1377

Blocking findings: none.

Non-blocking findings: none.

---
1. Replay-precedence closure — CLOSED

The added case missing_evaluation_and_watermark (tests/bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:2788-2797) has exactly the required shape:

- as_of_ms: Some(1_200) — snapshot/as-of present
- evaluation_receive_ms: None — evaluation receive time absent
- watermark_ms: None — receive watermark absent
- expected: BoltV3RvGateResult::MissingEvaluationEventTime

It executes through all three paths: the shared for case in cases loop calls public production classify_rv_gate (:2890) and asserts assert_eq!(production_result, case.expected, "{} must retain 
accepted_zero now persists a deliberately wrong stored gate of "rejected_stale"; the nine rejected cases retain the wrong value "accepted". Every case's stored gate is therefore wrong, so any fallback to it fails — on the accepted path as well as every rejected path.

I should be explicit: in my previous review I asserted that poisoning all cases to "accepted" meant "any reliance on the stored gate fails." That was wrong, and I missed it. For accepted_zero, whose expectation is Accepted, the poisoned value was the correct value — so a replay that simply returned the stored gate would have passed that case. The accepted path had no stored-gate independence proof. This correction closes it.

The new post-round-trip guard (:2958-2963) — assert_eq!(value.get("rv_gate_result"), Some(&json!(poisoned_stored_gate)), "{} must retain the deliberately wrong stored gate") — is placed after round_trip_decision_value / round_trip_evaluation_value, so it proves the wrong value actually survives serde into the record the replay sees, rather than being silently normalized away. Both poisoned strings round-trip validly: BoltV3ExitRvGateResult (bolt_v3_decision_evidence.rs:934) and BoltV3RvGateResult (:1709) both carry Accepted and RejectedStale under #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]. And the replay helper (:2470-2513) reads only rv_max_source_age_ms, rv_snapshot_has_ready_realized_vol / rv_ready, as-of, trigger_ts_init_ms, and the watermark — never the gate, never realized_vol.

3. Prior blocking closures — all reconfirmed

source_evidence.rs is byte-identical to 9122781f (git diff 9122781f 3afbb1527 -- .../tests/source_evidence.rs is empty), so both B2 closures carry over verbatim from the head I verified last round:

- Production/replay equivalence — now 10 table cases plus the above-i64::MAX case (:3039-3055), which builds a real snapshot at i64::MAX + 1, asserts production returns RejectedStale, and asserts decision replay equals high_production_result through i128. The fixture is a real RealizedVolSnapshot with three self-validating guards.
- A→B→A mask reset — rv_clock_domain_amendment_key_change_resets_every_seen_rv_bit marks Accepted+Some and RejectedStale+Some under key A, changes key, requires both to emit, returns to key A, requires both again, asserting the exact ordered six-record sequence on both entry-skip and blocked-snapshot paths. A mask-carrying mutant yields four records and fails.
- Same-key failed new-bit write — ..._blocked_snapshot_same_key_new_bit_failure_retries: bit 1 commits → bit 2's write fails → bit 1 stays suppressed and the writer is not reached (strategy_input_attempts() == 2) → bit 2 retries and emits.

4. Complete PR regression — reconfirmed

Production is byte-identical to the head I fully reviewed line-by-line (git diff 9122781f 3afbb1527 -- src/ ':!.../tests/' is empty), so that verification carries forward intact: receive-clock RV admission with event time retained as the computation/evidence coordinate; accepted-only causal watermarks derived from GridPoint sample identity across base/coarse/subsampled grids and maxed over every ready quorum source, with latest_accepted_event_ts excluding rejected observations; immutable entry and exit receipts, the exit one compact and captured before all eight early returns (mechanically enforced — it is a non-Option field set at construction); five checked i64::try_from conversions ordered before the lossless i128 delta narrowing; record-local non-aborting evidence failure (unit-returning, mark-before-failure, log-and-skip on both build and write error); startup-owned max-age policy via raw_taker_config plus the config-fields SSOT macro, with both obsolete runtime/context accessors and runtime_taker_pricing_config deleted and no dual path; bounded twelve-bit dedupe with correct writer atomicity; schema v15 held with both predeploy fixtures byte-identical; 1 MiB recovery boundary unchanged.

Scope is clean: zero changes to Cargo*, config/, .github/, ci/, justfile, crates/ — #1367's SBE pin (confirmed in the CI log as afc014a55b51463641cc19c68bffe25cdac6588a), #1371's cache protections, and #1375's PR-body governance are intact. The implementation plan is absent from the tree and the diff (only the design spec remains). No reference-price production correction (TakerPricingRequest deliberately retains a separate reference_gate_event_ms), no threshold or tolerance change, no S3 implementation, no #1275/#883/#763 work, no unrelated trading behavior.
